# Specification corpus review — consolidated findings

This report preserves the 6 September 2026 review of every document under
`specs/` against the working tree of branch `astra-review` at commit
`9f2e9576e0` plus the uncommitted edits present at review time. Findings and
line references describe that snapshot, not a fresh verification run; the
working tree was being edited while the review ran, so frontend-shared and
server-api findings in particular should be re-read against current files.
Delivery status lives in the [component roadmaps](../README.md); the test
coverage programme this report cites was retired later the same day. This
report is a record, not a work queue.

Method: 41 first-pass auditors read all 83 corpus files in full and checked
paths, symbols, links, governance rules, and the most consequential behavioural
claims against source. A second, independent pass of 37 refuters then tried to
knock down every finding. Codex was not used. The mechanical gate
(`tests/test_docs_accuracy.py`) was green on the snapshot, so every finding here
is semantic. Sibling artifacts: `findings.json` is the machine-readable ledger
(first-pass findings with second-pass verdicts, second-pass additions,
unverified claims, and the per-file coverage record) and
`spec-corpus-review.html` is the same content as a filterable page.

Working tree on branch astra-review at commit 9f2e9576 with uncommitted edits; 83 corpus files (32,177 markdown lines) read in full by 41 first-pass auditors; 234 raw findings; second-pass verdicts: confirmed 174, partial 46, refuted 14.

Standing findings: P1 7, P2 144, P3 69. Mechanical gate (tests/test_docs_accuracy.py, 61 tests) is green; every finding below is semantic.

## P1 findings

### F002 git-integration `specs/git-integration/low-level.md:105` accuracy
- Spec: **`GitWorkingBranchResponse.state`** (computed by `working_branch_status`) is one of six literal values: `"no-repository"` ... `"divergent"` ... or `"ready"`.
- Problem: The readiness route contract has seven states, not six. The spec omits `"git-unavailable"`, the state returned when no git binary is on PATH — a value the frontend explicitly branches on. An engineer implementing or consuming `GET /api/git/working-branch` from this spec would treat an unhandled value as impossible.
- Evidence: src/haute/schemas.py:2828-2836 defines `GitWorkingBranchState = Literal["git-unavailable", "no-repository", "unset", "detached", "invalid", "divergent", "ready"]` (seven). src/haute/_git_read_models.py:66-71 returns `GitWorkingBranchResponse(state="git-unavailable", ...)` when `git_binary_available()` is false. frontend/src/api/types.ts:1453 and frontend/src/components/BranchIndicator.tsx:166 handle that state; tests/test_git_engine.py:640 asserts it.
- Second pass: specs/git-integration/low-level.md:105 says the state "is one of six literal values" and enumerates six; src/haute/schemas.py:2828-2836 defines GitWorkingBranchState with seven literals including "git-unavailable", and src/haute/_git_read_models.py:66-71 returns state="git-unavailable" when git_binary_available() is false. The table row at line 116 repeats "six-state readiness contract", so the route contract is stated wrongly in two places.
- Fix: Change to seven literal values and document `"git-unavailable"` (no git binary on PATH; distinct from `"no-repository"` so the UI does not offer init), in both this paragraph and the `GET /api/git/working-branch` table row.

### F003 hosted-project-storage `specs/hosted-project-storage/low-level.md:43` accuracy
- Spec: It then hands the rest to `BindTask` (one background thread, one bind at a time) and returns **202** with `outcome: "pending"`.
- Problem: The bind route returns HTTP 200, not 202. The response body's `outcome` is indeed the literal "pending", but the status code stated in the spec is wrong, and it is stated in bold as a route contract an integrator would code against.
- Evidence: src/haute/routes/git.py:596 declares `@router.post("/storage/bind", response_model=GitBindStorageResponse)` with no `status_code=` argument, so FastAPI returns 200; `grep -n 202 src/haute/routes/git.py tests/test_storage_routes.py` finds no 202 anywhere in the route module or its tests.
- Second pass: specs/hosted-project-storage/low-level.md:43 states the bind route "returns **202** with `outcome: \"pending\"`". src/haute/routes/git.py:596 is `@router.post("/storage/bind", response_model=GitBindStorageResponse)` with no `status_code=`, so FastAPI returns 200; no `202` appears anywhere in the module. The body literal is right (src/haute/schemas.py:2937 `outcome: Literal["pending"] = "pending"`), so only the status code is wrong — but it is stated in bold as a route contract.
- Fix: Either change the sentence to "returns 200 with `outcome: \"pending\"`", or add `status_code=202` to the route decorator and pin it in tests/test_storage_routes.py.

### F004 hosted-project-storage `specs/hosted-project-storage/low-level.md:45` accuracy
- Spec: A `uc://` URL first acquires the claim (a live foreign holder → 409 with the holder record; the UI offers bind-elsewhere or fork), then branches on whether `HEAD.json` exists under the location
- Problem: The 409-on-claimed-location contract is stale from the pre-async bind design. Since the route now returns before any network work, a claimed `uc://` location cannot produce a 409 response; the failure is reported through the readiness response's `storage_bind` record instead. The same false claim appears twice more in this document.
- Evidence: src/haute/routes/git.py:596-627 (`git_bind_storage`) calls only `precheck_bind` and `bind_task().start(...)`; `precheck_bind` (src/haute/_project_storage.py) does URL validation, the already-bound refusal and the state-volume check only — it never touches the claim. `StorageClaimedError` does not appear anywhere in src/haute/routes/git.py, and the only 409s in that module are the milestone fork gate (line 289) and the push rejection (line 583). The claim is acquired inside the background `BindTask`, so the holder surfaces asynchronously on `storage_bind` (`GitStorageBind.claim`), never as a 409 from the bind call.
- Second pass: src/haute/routes/git.py:596-627 calls only `precheck_bind` (src/haute/_project_storage.py:824-846 — URL validation, already-bound refusal, state-volume check; it never touches the claim) and `bind_task().start(...)`, then returns. `StorageClaimedError` does not appear in routes/git.py at all; it is caught in `BindTask._run` (src/haute/_project_storage.py:640) and turned into `self._fail(url, str(exc), claim=exc.claim)`, i.e. a failed `storage_bind` record. So the "→ 409" on line 45 (and the same claim on lines 11 and 37) describes a contract the async route cannot produce.
- Fix: Delete the "→ 409" from line 45, from the Module map entry for `src/haute/routes/git.py` (line 11: "409 with the structured holder when the location is claimed"), and from the `StorageClaimedError` bullet (line 37: "→ HTTP 409 at bind"); replace with "surfaces as a failed `storage_bind` carrying the holder record".

### F006 sandbox-security `specs/sandbox-security/high-level.md:212` accuracy
- Spec: Haute has no reverse-proxy or shared-host mode because this UI can execute project code and access project files.
- Problem: A reverse-proxied, multi-user hosted deployment mode exists in the working tree and is the one place where this component's entire local-session gate is turned off. The security spec asserts the opposite as a hard product boundary, so a reader of the sandbox-security corpus never learns that a shipped code path disables the middleware this document is written about.
- Evidence: src/haute/hosted.py:1-20 module docstring: "Hosted-mode support: run haute behind a platform SSO reverse proxy... A hosted platform such as Databricks Apps inverts that boundary". src/haute/hosted.py:125-155 `create_app()` sets `os.environ[DISABLE_AUTH_ENV] = "1"` before importing `haute.server` and returns `PlatformProxyBoundary(server_app)`, which rewrites the Host header to loopback and strips `Forwarded`/`X-Forwarded-*` (src/haute/hosted.py:104-123) so `LocalTrustedHostMiddleware` accepts platform-proxied traffic. specs/hosted-databricks-app/low-level.md documents this component.
- Second pass: specs/sandbox-security/high-level.md:211-213 reads "Haute has no reverse-proxy or shared-host mode because this UI can execute project code and access project files." src/haute/hosted.py:1-21 documents exactly such a mode ("run haute behind a platform SSO reverse proxy"), and hosted.py:125-155 create_app() sets os.environ[DISABLE_AUTH_ENV]="1" then wraps the server app in PlatformProxyBoundary, which (hosted.py:107-122) strips Forwarded/X-Forwarded-*/Host and injects a 127.0.0.1 Host so LocalTrustedHostMiddleware accepts platform traffic. specs/hosted-databricks-app/{high,low}-level.md exist; the sandbox-security corpus never mentions hosted mode (grep for "hosted" across both files returns nothing). Security-boundary assertion contradicted by shipped code.
- Fix: Restate the boundary as it is: loopback-only is the posture of `haute serve`/the stock app, and the only exception is the explicit hosted deployment entry point in `src/haute/hosted.py`, which delegates authentication to a platform SSO proxy — cross-link ../hosted-databricks-app/high-level.md.

### F007 server-api `specs/server-api/low-level.md:601` stale
- Spec: Pipeline-list parse failures surface `str(exc)` as `PipelineSummary.error` only for `ParseError` (hand-authored, path-safe messages); any other exception is logged server-side and surfaced as a fixed "Failed to parse pipeline. Check the server logs for details." message
- Problem: `PipelineSummary` has no `error` field at all, and the model is `extra="forbid"`, so the described field cannot exist. There is also no `ParseError`-specific branch and no such fixed message anywhere in the source. The paragraph describes a `GET /api/pipelines` response contract the code does not implement, and contradicts this same document's own endpoint table (line 181).
- Evidence: src/haute/schemas.py:1310-1318 defines `PipelineSummary` with `model_config = ConfigDict(extra="forbid")` and exactly `name, description, file, node_count, load_status, diagnostic_count` — no `error`. src/haute/routes/pipeline.py:586-613 `_parse_one` catches a bare `Exception` and returns `PipelineSummary(..., load_status="source_only", diagnostic_count=1)`. `grep -rn "Failed to parse pipeline" src/haute/ --include=*.py` returns nothing.
- Second pass: Quote is verbatim but lives at specs/server-api/low-level.md:613-616, not 601 (601 is the TraceCorrelationUnsupportedError row). Substance holds: src/haute/schemas.py PipelineSummary is ConfigDict(extra="forbid") with only name/description/file/node_count/load_status/diagnostic_count; src/haute/routes/pipeline.py:586-613 _parse_one catches bare Exception and returns load_status="source_only", diagnostic_count=1; grep for "Failed to parse pipeline" in src/haute returns nothing. Public GET /api/pipelines contract described wrongly.
- Fix: Replace the paragraph with the shipped behaviour: any load failure for a discovered file yields a `source_only` summary with `diagnostic_count=1` and the failure is logged server-side; there is no per-item error string on the wire.

### F008 submodels `specs/submodels/high-level.md:373` stale
- Spec: Depends on the codegen component through `SavePipelineService`: create emits the rewritten parent plus submodel files; dissolve uses `graph_to_code` only when no submodels remain and `graph_to_code_multi` when other occurrences remain.
- Problem: Both submodel-mutating routes are transform-only and call no codegen at all. This bullet asserts that create writes the parent and submodel files, contradicting the same document's Behaviour section ('It does not write the parent, module, config, or sidecar') and the code.
- Evidence: src/haute/routes/submodel.py contains no reference to graph_to_code or graph_to_code_multi (grep over the file returns only ConfigError/ParseError imports at line 18). create_submodel (src/haute/routes/submodel.py:118-214) only parses, transforms via create_submodel_graph, calls svc.validate_new_module_files (a read-only no-clobber check, src/haute/routes/_save_pipeline.py:475-477) and returns a response; dissolve_submodel (src/haute/routes/submodel.py:295-358) only calls flatten_graph and returns.
- Second pass: specs/submodels/high-level.md:372-375 says "create emits the rewritten parent plus submodel files; dissolve uses `graph_to_code` ... `graph_to_code_multi`". grep over src/haute/routes/submodel.py finds no graph_to_code/graph_to_code_multi; the only SavePipelineService use is the read-only svc.validate_new_module_files at src/haute/routes/submodel.py:190-192, and dissolve only calls flatten_graph. This also contradicts the same document's own bullet at high-level.md:334-337 ("Submodel endpoints only validate and transform graphs").
- Fix: Rewrite the bullet as a dependency of the explicit Save transaction only: `SavePipelineService` (server-api) invokes codegen; the submodel routes neither generate nor write code.

### F009 submodels `specs/submodels/high-level.md:437` accuracy
- Spec: A null target handle on an inbound submodel edge is an unassigned editor draft and is omitted by `flatten_graph`.
- Problem: flatten_graph does not omit such an edge; it raises ParseError. Any inbound parent edge whose targetHandle is None and whose target is a SUBMODEL occurrence fails validation before any output is built, so dissolve returns 400 and preview/trace/execution fail rather than continuing.
- Evidence: src/haute/_flatten.py:20 calls resolve_submodel_instances(graph) first. src/haute/_submodel_instances.py:184-191 iterates every edge whose target is a resolved occurrence and calls _input_port -> _port_id (src/haute/_submodel_instances.py:213-228), which raises ParseError('Submodel boundary edge has a missing or malformed public-port handle.') when `not handle`. No filter for null handles exists in _flatten.py (29 lines, read in full) or in _submodel_instances.py; no test in tests/test_flatten.py, tests/test_submodel_instances.py or tests/test_submodel.py exercises a null targetHandle.
- Second pass: specs/submodels/high-level.md:437 quoted verbatim. src/haute/_flatten.py (29 lines, no null-handle filter) calls resolve_submodel_instances first; src/haute/_submodel_instances.py:191-192 calls _input_port for every edge whose target is a resolved occurrence, and _port_id (src/haute/_submodel_instances.py:213-228) raises ParseError('Submodel boundary edge has a missing or malformed public-port handle.') when `not handle`. So a null targetHandle raises rather than being omitted, breaking the dissolve route contract (400 instead of success).
- Fix: Either state that a null inbound handle raises a contextual ParseError (matching _port_id), or implement the drop-the-draft behaviour before resolve_submodel_instances. Update the matching low-level Error handling row (specs/submodels/low-level.md:425) in the same edit.

## P2 findings

### F011 assistant `specs/assistant/high-level.md:43` consistency
- Spec: The assistant HTTP surface: session create, readiness/status, and the message endpoint that streams a turn as server-sent events.
- Problem: The enumerated HTTP surface omits `GET /api/assistant/sessions`, a real public route that returns this pipeline's saved conversations for the panel's chat list. The Scope section reads as a complete enumeration and the Failure model likewise never mentions it, so a reader takes the surface to be three endpoints when it is four. The component's own low-level.md documents the fourth.
- Evidence: src/haute/routes/assistant.py:203-204 `@router.get("/sessions", response_model=AssistantSessionListResponse)` / `async def list_assistant_sessions(pipeline: str | None = None)`; specs/assistant/low-level.md:32 and :436 both document `GET /api/assistant/sessions`.
- Second pass: specs/assistant/high-level.md:43-44 enumerates only session create, readiness/status and the message endpoint, while src/haute/routes/assistant.py declares four routes (:152 /status, :203 /sessions, :237 /session, :340 /message). `grep -n "sessions" specs/assistant/high-level.md` finds only on-disk session-file mentions (94, 402), never the list endpoint, whereas specs/assistant/low-level.md:32 and :436 document it.
- Fix: Add the session-list endpoint to the Scope bullet at line 43 and, if it has distinct failure behaviour, to the Failure model.

### F012 assistant `specs/assistant/high-level.md:76` governance (partial)
- Spec: the nested table requires the exact trust/sensitivity/`allow_*` fields defined in the ASSIST-A07 contract below.
- Problem: The document points the reader at an "ASSIST-A07 contract below" that does not exist. The document has no `## Approved change contract` section (grep for `Approved change contract` returns nothing in either assistant spec), and no ASSIST-A07 package exists in any roadmap file. The contract was folded but the cross-reference to it was left behind, so the authoritative definition of the five egress fields appears to live somewhere it does not.
- Evidence: `grep -rn "ASSIST-A07" --include=*.md .` returns only specs/assistant/high-level.md:76 and specs/assistant/low-level.md:814; `grep -n "Approved change contract" specs/assistant/*.md` returns nothing; `ls specs/roadmap/` contains background-jobs-api.md, bug-findings-2026-09-05.md, engineering-quality.md, explore-eda.md, optimiser.md, README.md, test-gap-findings-2026-09-05.md — no assistant roadmap file and no ASSIST-A07 package anywhere.
- Second pass: The dead reference is real: high-level.md:76 says the nested fields are "defined in the ASSIST-A07 contract below" and no ASSIST-A07 package exists in specs/roadmap/ (only test docstrings, e.g. tests/test_assistant_project_knowledge.py:1, carry that ID — and for project knowledge, not egress config). But the finding overstates: the five fields ARE defined below, in the `## Egress and project knowledge` section at high-level.md:657-664, so the definition does not "live somewhere it does not"; only the package label is unresolvable.
- Fix: Drop the ASSIST-A07 reference and state the five required `[assistant.egress]` fields inline (they are already listed at lines 660-664), or restore a real anchored roadmap link if an active package still owns this.

### F013 assistant `specs/assistant/high-level.md:184` accuracy
- Spec: For a routed Parquet showcase whose request explicitly names a safe relative folder, an omitted listing root is bound to that folder and recursive discovery is enabled, so datasets below folders such as `data/claims/` are not falsely reported as absent.
- Problem: This is described as behaviour of the `list_datasets` tool, but no code binds an omitted listing root or enables recursion. The router only appends an English instruction to the system prompt telling the model to pass those arguments itself; the tool executor receives whatever the model sends. The spec also contradicts itself at lines 605-607 ("No lexical result ... populates an argument, rewrites a tool call").
- Evidence: src/haute/assistant/_loop.py:450-454 appends prose: "The user explicitly named dataset directory `{dataset_root}`. Call `list_datasets` with `project_root` = `{dataset_root}` and `recursive` = true." `grep -rn "explicit_dataset_directory|dataset_root" src/haute/assistant/*.py` shows the helper is used only in _loop.py's prompt assembly and defined at src/haute/assistant/_recipes.py:391; src/haute/assistant/_tools.py contains no root-binding or recursion default for the showcase route.
- Second pass: src/haute/assistant/_tools.py:873-902 `list_datasets` takes `project_root`/`recursive` verbatim (default `recursive=False`, `project_root or "."`), and the dispatcher at :1786-1791 passes `arguments.get("recursive", False)` unchanged; the only occurrence of the behaviour is prose appended to the system prompt at src/haute/assistant/_loop.py:450-453. This also contradicts high-level.md:605-607 ("No lexical result ... populates an argument").
- Fix: Move this sentence out of the `list_datasets` tool description and state it as advisory route guidance in the system prompt, matching lines 605-607.

### F150 assistant `specs/assistant/high-level.md:299` governance
- Spec: a user decision, 2026-07-19: the earlier optional-extra packaging optimised for a lean default install, but a feature the product leads with must not require a second install step
- Problem: R1 forbids resolved history in a spec. This sentence, and the matching one at line 401 ("a user decision, 2026-07-19, reversing the earlier in-memory-only stance"), describe superseded states of the code rather than the code as it is. Rejected *alternatives* belong in Design rationale; a previous shipped implementation does not.
- Evidence: Both claims are about prior repository states, not current behaviour: src/haute/assistant/_config.py:33-37 shows the SDKs as unconditional core requirements with no extras branch, and src/haute/assistant/_session.py:57-60 shows write-through persistence (`MAX_LIVE_SESSIONS`/`MAX_PERSISTED_SESSIONS`) with no in-memory-only mode to reverse.
- Second pass: high-level.md:299-300 ("the earlier optional-extra packaging optimised for a lean default install") and :401 ("reversing the earlier in-memory-only stance") both narrate superseded repository states as such, which is exactly the resolved-history the writing rules forbid (TEMPLATE.md:72-76 requires folding resolved history into present-tense prose); the constraints themselves survive without the history. Severity is understated at P3 — the rubric places legacy/history language at P2.
- Fix: State the constraint without the history: "The SDKs ship in the core `haute` dependencies because a feature the product leads with must not require a second install step"; likewise drop "reversing the earlier in-memory-only stance" at line 401.

### F014 assistant `specs/assistant/high-level.md:594` accuracy
- Spec: With two to eight discovered Parquet datasets, that showcase route inspects every schema and ranks coherent pairs deterministically: a shared `quote_id` outranks a pair with exactly one other shared column, then larger combined distinct schema width wins, then project-relative path order breaks a tie.
- Problem: The ranking rule is presented as deterministic backend behaviour performed by "the showcase route", but it exists only as natural-language guidance appended to the provider system prompt. Nothing in the backend inspects schemas or ranks pairs; the model does, and may not comply. An engineer would expect a deterministic, unit-testable selection routine that does not exist.
- Evidence: src/haute/assistant/_loop.py:438-448 is the only occurrence: the entire ranking rule is a string appended to `route_guidance`. `grep -rn "quote_id" src/haute/assistant/*.py` matches only src/haute/assistant/_loop.py:443 (the prompt string) — there is no schema-inspection or pair-ranking code path anywhere in the assistant package.
- Second pass: src/haute/assistant/_loop.py:440-448 is the whole implementation: the shared-`quote_id`/one-shared-column/combined-width/path-order rule is a string appended to `route_guidance`. `grep -rn "quote_id" src/haute/assistant/*.py` matches only that prompt string, so no backend schema inspection or pair ranking exists, while high-level.md:593-596 attributes the deterministic ranking to "that showcase route".
- Fix: Reword as "the route's advisory guidance asks the model to rank..." (consistent with lines 605-609), or implement the ranking in the recipe planner if determinism is intended.

### F016 assistant `specs/assistant/low-level.md:814` stale
- Spec: The outer accepted key set is exactly `provider`, `model`, `base_url`, and `egress`; the nested table has exactly the five required ASSIST-A07 fields.
- Problem: ASSIST-A07 does not resolve to any roadmap package, so the reference is dead. The five fields are in any case enumerable directly and are already stated in the Module map row for `_config.py`.
- Evidence: No specs/roadmap/assistant.md exists; `grep -rn "ASSIST-A07" specs/roadmap/*.md` returns nothing. The actual closed nested key set is `src/haute/assistant/_config.py:46-50` — trust, max_sensitivity, allow_project_knowledge, allow_executable_source, allow_row_samples.
- Second pass: specs/assistant/low-level.md:813-814 defers the nested key set to "the five required ASSIST-A07 fields"; `grep -rn "ASSIST-A0" specs/roadmap/*.md` returns nothing and specs/roadmap/ holds no assistant file, so the identifier resolves nowhere authoritative (the only in-repo ASSIST-A07 is tests/test_assistant_project_knowledge.py:1, a different subject). The five names are trivially statable, as high-level.md:659-664 already does.
- Fix: Replace "the five required ASSIST-A07 fields" with the five field names themselves.

### F017 assistant `specs/assistant/low-level.md:964` stale
- Spec: ASSIST-A05 adds canonical revision/plan hashing, semantic diff boundaries, closed postconditions, single-use plan transitions, stale/altered-plan rejection before save, unrelated-diff detection, and truthful verification evidence.
- Problem: The spec cites roadmap package IDs ASSIST-A05 (line 964) and ASSIST-A04 (line 970) that exist nowhere in the roadmap corpus, and phrases them as delivery history ("adds", "additionally pins") rather than present-tense description of current test coverage. A reader cannot resolve the package to decide whether the work is active or retired.
- Evidence: `ls specs/roadmap/` returns background-jobs-api.md, bug-findings-2026-09-05.md, engineering-quality.md, explore-eda.md, optimiser.md, README.md, test-gap-findings-2026-09-05.md — there is no specs/roadmap/assistant.md. `grep -rn "ASSIST-A0[457]" specs/roadmap/*.md` returns no matches.
- Second pass: specs/assistant/low-level.md:964 ("ASSIST-A05 adds ...") and :970 ("ASSIST-A04 additionally pins ...") cite package IDs absent from every specs/roadmap/*.md file; they survive only as a code comment (src/haute/assistant/_catalog.py:266) and test docstrings (tests/test_assistant_application.py:1). The "adds"/"additionally pins" framing narrates delivery history rather than describing the current coverage of the named test modules.
- Fix: Drop the package IDs and rewrite both bullets in present tense describing what tests/test_assistant_ops.py and tests/test_assistant_catalog.py currently cover (e.g. "Also covers canonical revision/plan hashing, ...").

### F018 background-jobs `specs/background-jobs/low-level.md:59` accuracy
- Spec: ### `_job_lifecycle.py`

- **`TerminalReason`** — `Literal[...]`. - **`TERMINAL_REASONS`** — the closed terminal-status set. ... - **`_TERMINAL_REASON_PRECEDENCE`** — `error=10 < contract_error=20 < memory_limited=30 < cancelled=40 < timed_out=50 < superseded=60`.
- Problem: The Key-types section attributes `TerminalReason`, `TERMINAL_REASONS` and `_TERMINAL_REASON_PRECEDENCE` to `src/haute/routes/_job_lifecycle.py`. All three are defined in `src/haute/routes/_job_store.py` and only imported by the lifecycle facade. An engineer changing the precedence policy or the terminal vocabulary would open the wrong file.

### F019 background-jobs `specs/background-jobs/low-level.md:366` accuracy
- Spec: Any other `BaseException` becomes an `error` outcome with a bounded generic message plus `worker_error_class`/`supervisor_error_class`; the original exception is retained as `exception_to_report`.

- Fix: Change to "plus `supervisor_error_class` (no `worker_error_class`, which only typed worker failures carry)".

### F024 caching `specs/caching/high-level.md:55` accuracy
- Spec: there is no cancel endpoint because the underlying blocking shred is not cooperatively cancellable.
- Problem: The stated rationale is contradicted by the code: the structured-input build is explicitly cooperatively cancellable. It runs through a cancellation-gated isolated worker transaction, so request cancellation (client disconnect) sets a gate, stops the worker with terminal reason "cancelled", and discards the staging directory. The absence of a cancel *route* is true; the reason given is not. `specs/caching/low-level.md:123` repeats the same framing and line 118-119 describes the work only as "Blocking shred work runs with a response timeout", omitting the isolated-worker/cancellation-gate machinery entirely.
- Evidence: src/haute/routes/json_cache.py:190-250 passes a `WorkerCancellationGate` into `_json_cache_build_transaction`, raising `IsolatedWorkerStoppedError(terminal_reason="cancelled")` when set and wiring `stop_reason=(lambda: "cancelled" if cancellation_requested.is_set() else None)` into `worker_config_for_memory_policy`; json_cache.py:620 awaits `run_cancellable_worker_transaction`, defined at src/haute/routes/_isolated_worker_async.py:46-77, which on `asyncio.CancelledError` calls `cancellation_requested.request()` and drains the worker.
- Second pass: specs/caching/high-level.md:54-55 states the quoted rationale verbatim, and specs/caching/low-level.md:123 repeats "There is no backend cancel operation". src/haute/routes/json_cache.py:197-249 threads a `WorkerCancellationGate` into `_json_cache_build_transaction` (checks `cancellation_requested.is_set()`, passes `stop_reason=lambda: "cancelled" ...`, `publication_guard()`), and json_cache.py:610-620 awaits `run_cancellable_worker_transaction`, which at src/haute/routes/_isolated_worker_async.py:58-77 calls `cancellation_requested.request()` on `asyncio.CancelledError` and drains the worker, raising `IsolatedWorkerStoppedError(terminal_reason="cancelled")`. The shred work is therefore cooperatively cancellable; only the absence of a cancel route is true.
- Fix: Replace the rationale with the shipped behaviour: no cancel endpoint is exposed; the build is cancelled cooperatively by request cancellation through the isolated-worker cancellation gate, which stops the worker and discards staging. Update the low-level control-flow step and the "no backend cancel operation" sentence to match.

### F025 caching `specs/caching/high-level.md:80` governance
- Spec: warned before and recorded in the terminal diagnostics, never silently — so Studio no longer prompts for it; the explicit refresh action remains available.
- Problem: R1 violation: "no longer prompts" describes resolved history (a prompt that used to exist) instead of the current behaviour. The spec should state only what the code does now.
- Evidence: Governance rule R1 / specs/TEMPLATE.md:72-76 forbids resolved-history phrasing; the surrounding claims (lazy_sink first, one retry on `snapshot_build_unsupported`) are accurate per frontend/src/hooks/ensureInputSnapshots.ts:86-99, so only the historical clause is at fault.
- Second pass: specs/caching/high-level.md:79-80 reads "...never silently — so Studio no longer prompts for it; the explicit refresh action remains available." "no longer prompts" narrates a superseded state, which specs/TEMPLATE.md:72-76 forbids ("fold resolved history into present-tense prose"). Only the historical clause is at fault; the surrounding behaviour claims are not disputed.
- Fix: State it positively: automatic preparation refreshes a ready-but-stale snapshot before the run, warning beforehand and recording it in the terminal diagnostics; Studio prompts only for missing, corrupt, failed, or building snapshots, and the explicit refresh action remains available.

### F026 caching `specs/caching/high-level.md:121` missing
- Spec: JSON schema and parse failures return structured 4xx responses; timeouts return 504; unexpected failures are logged and return a generic 500.
- Problem: The Failure model (and the low-level Error handling paragraph at line 165-166) omits two live response codes of the structured-input cache route: HTTP 507 for worker/admission memory-limit failures and HTTP 409 for a source that changed during the build or a stopped worker. A reader relying on this spec would not expect either status from `/api/json-cache/build`.
- Evidence: src/haute/routes/json_cache.py:640-674 — `exc.kind == "source_changed"` raises `HTTPException(status_code=409, detail="The structured source changed during the cache build; retry the build.")`, `exc.kind == "memory"` raises 507, `IsolatedWorkerMemoryLimitExceededError`/`IsolatedWorkerMemoryLimitUnsupportedError` raise 507 with `_isolated_memory_detail(...)` (json_cache.py:263-288), `IsolatedWorkerCrashedError` with `terminal_reason == "memory_limited"` raises 507, and `IsolatedWorkerStoppedError` raises 409.
- Second pass: specs/caching/high-level.md:120-121 lists only structured 4xx, 504 and generic 500, and specs/caching/low-level.md:165-166 says the routes "return 504 on response timeout, and log unexpected errors before a generic 500". src/haute/routes/json_cache.py:640-680 additionally raises 409 for `exc.kind == "source_changed"` and for `IsolatedWorkerStoppedError`, and 507 for `exc.kind == "memory"`, `IsolatedWorkerMemoryLimitExceededError`/`Unsupported`, and `IsolatedWorkerCrashedError` with `terminal_reason == "memory_limited"` (detail built at json_cache.py:263-288). Both status families are live and unspecced.
- Fix: Add the 507 memory-limit family (worker RSS limit, native cap unavailable, memory-limited crash, admission rejection) and the 409 source-changed/worker-stopped responses to the Failure model and to the low-level Error handling and boundary failure ordering.

### F028 caching `specs/caching/low-level.md:149` governance
- Spec: Consulting only `working/` (the volatile layer) reported `cached=False` whenever it was absent or stale-fingerprinted while `committed/` — the durable layer that survives a restart — was valid and still serving every run, inviting a rebuild of a cache already in use. Precedence is unchanged: a valid `working/` still wins
- Problem: R1 violation: the invariant narrates resolved history in the past tense ("reported", "inviting a rebuild", "Precedence is unchanged") rather than describing the current code as-is. The shipped behaviour — status resolves working/ then committed/, and `cached=False` requires both layers invalid — is correct, but the fix narrative must be folded into present-tense prose.
- Evidence: src/haute/routes/json_cache.py:759-766 implements the current behaviour with `for layer in ("working", "committed")`; the surrounding comment carries the same historical narrative. Nothing in the code corresponds to the past-tense "reported" state, so the spec is describing a superseded implementation.
- Second pass: specs/caching/low-level.md:148-153 contains the quoted past-tense narrative ("reported `cached=False` ... inviting a rebuild of a cache already in use. Precedence is unchanged"), which is resolved history rather than present-tense description (specs/TEMPLATE.md:72-76). The current behaviour it wraps is correct: src/haute/routes/json_cache.py:759-771 loops `for layer in ("working", "committed")` and returns `cached=False` only when both layers are invalid.
- Fix: Rewrite the bullet in present tense: status resolves `working/` then `committed/`, matching `load_v2_api_source`'s run-time order; a valid `working/` wins and `cached=False` requires both layers to be invalid. Drop the account of the previous single-layer behaviour.

### F029 cli `specs/cli/low-level.md:109` accuracy
- Spec: `handle_serve` runs, in order: `_require_loopback_host` ..., `_configure_trusted_hosts` ..., `_abort_if_port_in_use` (pre-flight socket bind/close probe ...), then `_detect_dev_frontend_dir` to choose dev vs. prod mode.
- Problem: The stated ordering is reversed for the last two steps. `handle_serve` selects the frontend directory before any port probing, so a frontend-detection failure surfaces before a port-conflict error, not after.
- Evidence: src/haute/cli/_serve.py:584-594 — `_require_loopback_host(config)`, `_configure_trusted_hosts(config)`, `frontend_dir = _detect_dev_frontend_dir()`, and only then `_abort_if_port_in_use(config)` inside each branch (plus `_abort_if_vite_port_in_use()` in the dev branch).
- Second pass: specs/cli/low-level.md:109-113 states the order as `_require_loopback_host` → `_configure_trusted_hosts` → `_abort_if_port_in_use` → `_detect_dev_frontend_dir`. src/haute/cli/_serve.py:584-594 shows `frontend_dir = _detect_dev_frontend_dir()` on line 586, with `_abort_if_port_in_use(config)` called only afterwards inside each branch (plus `_abort_if_vite_port_in_use()` in dev), so the last two steps are reversed exactly as the finding says (the separate invariant at line 208-211, which only asserts loopback-before-probe, remains true).
- Fix: Reorder the sentence: `_require_loopback_host` → `_configure_trusted_hosts` → `_detect_dev_frontend_dir` → `_abort_if_port_in_use` (plus `_abort_if_vite_port_in_use` in dev mode).

### F030 cli `specs/cli/low-level.md:127` accuracy
- Spec: It schedules a background thread that polls both the backend TCP port and Vite's fixed TCP port and opens the browser only once both are accepting connections (`_open_browser_after_backend_ready` → `_wait_for_tcp_ready`)
- Problem: The named symbol is the wrong one. `_open_browser_after_backend_ready` waits only on the backend port; the both-ports behaviour the sentence describes belongs to `_open_browser_after_servers_ready`, which is what dev mode actually calls. An engineer following the citation would edit the unused single-port helper.
- Evidence: src/haute/cli/_serve.py:485-490 — `_run_dev_mode` calls `_open_browser_after_servers_ready(...)`. `_open_browser_after_servers_ready` (line 409) targets `_wait_for_servers_then_open_browser` (line 371), which awaits `_VITE_HOST:_VITE_PORT` at line 377; `_open_browser_after_backend_ready` (line 393) targets `_wait_for_backend_then_open_browser` (line 345) and is not called from `_run_dev_mode`.
- Second pass: specs/cli/low-level.md:125-128 attributes the both-ports wait to `_open_browser_after_backend_ready` → `_wait_for_tcp_ready`. src/haute/cli/_serve.py:485 shows `_run_dev_mode` calling `_open_browser_after_servers_ready`, which targets `_wait_for_servers_then_open_browser` (line 371) awaiting both the backend and `_VITE_HOST:_VITE_PORT` (line 377); `_open_browser_after_backend_ready` (line 393) waits on the backend only and is not called from dev mode.
- Fix: Replace the parenthetical with `_open_browser_after_servers_ready` → `_wait_for_servers_then_open_browser` → `_wait_for_tcp_ready`.

### F163 cli `specs/cli/low-level.md:174` accuracy
- Spec: resolves the staging suffix (CLI flag wins, else `deploy_config.ci.staging_endpoint_suffix`, else loud error)
- Problem: The "else loud error" branch is conditional on the Databricks transport. For HTTP and unsupported targets an absent suffix is not an error at all, so the spec overstates the invariant.
- Evidence: src/haute/cli/_impact.py:78 — `if transport.kind == "databricks" and not staging_suffix:` guards the `SystemExit(1)`; for other kinds `staging_name = base_name + (staging_suffix or "")` (line 88) proceeds silently.
- Second pass: specs/cli/low-level.md:175-176 states the suffix resolution ends in "else loud error" unconditionally. src/haute/cli/_impact.py:78 guards the `SystemExit(1)` with `if transport.kind == "databricks" and not staging_suffix:`; for http/unsupported transports line 88 computes `staging_name = base_name + (staging_suffix or "")`, silently yielding `staging_name == prod_name` — the exact outcome the surrounding code comment claims is prevented. This is a factual inaccuracy about behaviour, not mere ambiguity, so P2.
- Fix: Qualify the loud-error clause as Databricks-only, and note that on other transports an empty suffix silently yields `staging_name == prod_name`.

### F031 codegen `specs/codegen/high-level.md:162` accuracy
- Spec: API Input and External File bodies contain only their sidecar path and call the shared config-driven loaders with `Path(__file__).resolve().parent` as the pipeline-directory candidate.
- Problem: The generated bodies do not pass `Path(__file__).resolve().parent`; they pass the module-level `_HAUTE_CONFIG_BASE`, which in a submodel file is `Path(__file__).resolve().parents[N]`. The claim contradicts the low-level document's own config-base paragraph, and would mislead anyone reasoning about how a nested submodel resolves its sidecar.
- Evidence: src/haute/_codegen_builders.py:244-246 emits `resolve_api_input_from_config(<cfg>, base_dir=_HAUTE_CONFIG_BASE)`; _RETAINED_EXTERNAL at src/haute/_codegen_builders.py:331-334 emits `load_external_object_from_config(<cfg>, base_dir=_HAUTE_CONFIG_BASE)`. `grep -n "__file__" src/haute/_codegen_builders.py` returns no matches. The base is defined in src/haute/codegen.py:633-640 as `_HautePath(__file__).resolve().parents[<depth>]` for submodel files and `.parent` only for pipeline files.
- Second pass: specs/codegen/high-level.md:160-162 quotes exactly as cited. No generated body contains `__file__`: `grep -n "__file__" src/haute/_codegen_builders.py` returns nothing, and the API-input/external-file bodies emit `base_dir=_HAUTE_CONFIG_BASE` (src/haute/_codegen_builders.py:246, 333). `_HAUTE_CONFIG_BASE` is `.parent` only for pipeline files; a submodel file gets `_HautePath(__file__).resolve().parents[N]` (src/haute/codegen.py:624-636), so the spec sentence is wrong for submodel-hosted nodes.
- Fix: Say that both builders pass the module-level `_HAUTE_CONFIG_BASE` (the parent pipeline directory, `.parent` in a pipeline file and `.parents[N]` in a submodel file) as the base directory.

### F034 codegen `specs/codegen/low-level.md:220` stale
- Spec: Modules generated before this contract carry a codegen-prepended `df = <first input>` alias line (or a passthrough `return <input>` body); on reload these round-trip into the code box as visible, explicit user code (`df = <input>`) — which preserves the old behaviour exactly, since the code now performs the binding the runtime no longer injects.
- Problem: This describes a backward-compatibility/legacy reload path for previously generated modules and uses resolved-history language ("before this contract", "the old behaviour", "no longer injects"). Haute is prerelease and each boundary accepts exactly its canonical format, so a described compatibility path for older generated files is a governance violation.
- Evidence: The current code has no legacy branch: src/haute/_codegen_builders.py:948-956 emits only `POLARS_OUTPUT_DECLARATION` plus the user body, and src/haute/_code_extraction.py:496-529 (`_match_polars`) recognises only the current placeholder and the exact unbound output declaration — a `df = <param>` line is plain user code with no alias handling.
- Second pass: specs/codegen/low-level.md:219-223 carries the quoted sentence verbatim ("Modules generated before this contract ... the runtime no longer injects"), describing a legacy-reload compatibility path in resolved-history language. The code has no such branch: src/haute/_codegen_builders.py:948-956 emits only POLARS_OUTPUT_DECLARATION plus the user body, and the polars matcher/finaliser treat `df = <param>` as authored code with no alias handling.
- Fix: Delete the legacy sentence and keep only the present-tense statement that a `df = <param>` line in a polars body is authored user code, never scaffold.

### F032 codegen `specs/codegen/high-level.md:288` governance
- Spec: **Shares** `src/haute/_ast_helpers.py` and `src/haute/_code_extraction.py` with the parser (`src/haute/parser.py`, `src/haute/_graph_builders.py`, `src/haute/_parser_regex.py`, `src/haute/_parser_submodels.py`)

Only `_python_syntax.py` and `_registry.py` from this component's map are recorded.

### F037 databricks-io `specs/databricks-io/low-level.md:8` governance (partial)
- Spec: It re-exports `DatabricksConfigError` for compatibility.
- Problem: R1 forbids describing a compatibility/alias path as current in a prerelease codebase; the re-export exists only because the module imports the error to raise it, not as a compatibility shim. The phrase reads as a legacy import alias that must be preserved.
- Evidence: src/haute/_databricks_io.py:17-20 imports `DatabricksConfigError` from `haute._databricks_credentials` and raises it at lines 92 and 182; there is no `__all__` alias or deprecated-import path in the file.
- Second pass: specs/databricks-io/low-level.md:8 does read "It re-exports `DatabricksConfigError` for compatibility.", and the "for compatibility" framing is a backward-compatibility path described as current, which the prerelease/canonical-only rule forbids — that part is confirmed. But the supporting evidence overstates: the re-export is real and consumed (tests/test_databricks_io.py:11-13 imports `DatabricksConfigError` from `haute._databricks_io`), so it is not merely an incidental import, and the suggested fix's claim that line 23 also says "for compatibility" is wrong (line 23 reads "...owned by `_databricks_credentials.py` and re-exported by `_databricks_io.py`", accurate prose). Only the two words "for compatibility" are the defect.
- Fix: Replace with plain present-tense prose, e.g. "It raises `DatabricksConfigError` from `src/haute/_databricks_credentials.py`", and drop "for compatibility" from line 23 as well.

### F038 databricks-io `specs/databricks-io/low-level.md:42` accuracy
- Spec: Each fetch is preceded by a checkpoint. Transient exceptions retry at most three times with exponential backoff.
- Problem: The retry budget is three total attempts, i.e. at most two retries, not three retries. An engineer sizing the worst-case fetch latency or writing a retry test from this sentence would expect one more retry and one more backoff sleep than the code performs.
- Evidence: src/haute/_databricks_io.py:227-238: `for attempt in range(_FETCH_MAX_RETRIES)` with `_FETCH_MAX_RETRIES = 3` (line 160); the handler re-raises when `attempt == _FETCH_MAX_RETRIES - 1`, so attempts 0 and 1 sleep and retry (backoff 1.0s then 2.0s) and attempt 2 propagates.
- Second pass: specs/databricks-io/low-level.md:42-43 says "Transient exceptions retry at most three times with exponential backoff"; src/haute/_databricks_io.py:160 sets `_FETCH_MAX_RETRIES = 3` and lines 227-238 use `for attempt in range(_FETCH_MAX_RETRIES)` re-raising when `attempt == _FETCH_MAX_RETRIES - 1`, so there are three total attempts and only two retries (sleeps of 1.0s and 2.0s). The spec overstates the retry budget by one attempt/backoff.
- Fix: Reword to "a fetch is attempted at most three times (two retries) with exponential backoff of 1s then 2s".

- Second pass: specs/databricks-io/low-level.md:70 writes `routes/databricks.py` and line 23 writes bare `_databricks_credentials.py` / `_databricks_io.py`, while specs/TEMPLATE.md:80-81 requires "an exact repository-root-relative path such as `src/haute/parser.py`" and "Do not use an ambiguous basename when a path is available"; the real files are src/haute/routes/databricks.py, src/haute/_databricks_credentials.py, src/haute/_databricks_io.py (all named root-relative in the same document's module map, lines 7-9). Confirmed as a rule violation; severity is better set at P2 (explicit writing-rule breach) than the finding's P3.

### F040 deploy `specs/deploy/low-level.md:36` missing
- Spec: **`ResolvedDeploy`** (`src/haute/deploy/_config.py`) — the target-agnostic handoff object, created only by `resolve_config()`: `config`, `full_graph`, `pruned_graph`, `input_node_ids`, `output_node_id`, `artifacts` (`dict[str, Path]`), `input_schema`/`output_schema` ..., `execution_policy` ..., `removed_node_ids`.
- Problem: The field list omits `snapshot_provenance` and the resource-lifetime surface (`_resources` ExitStack, `close()`, and the `__enter__`/`__exit__` context-manager protocol). The lease lifetime the spec describes in prose is actually enforced by `close()`, which `deploy()`/`deploy_resolved()` call in a `finally`; a reader of the Key types section cannot see that the object must be closed.
- Evidence: src/haute/deploy/_config.py:499 `snapshot_provenance: dict[str, dict[str, Any]]`; :500 `_resources: ExitStack`; :503 `def close(self)`; :510-513 `__enter__`/`__exit__`. src/haute/deploy/__init__.py:95 and :117 call `resolved.close()` in `finally`.
- Second pass: specs/deploy/low-level.md:36-41 lists ResolvedDeploy fields ending at `removed_node_ids`; src/haute/deploy/_config.py:499 declares `snapshot_provenance`, :500 `_resources: ExitStack`, :503 `close()`, :510-513 `__enter__`/`__exit__`, and src/haute/deploy/__init__.py:99 and :117 call `resolved.close()` in `finally`. Neither spec file mentions `snapshot_provenance` or `close()` anywhere (grep over both files), so the type description is materially incomplete for a resource-owning object.
- Fix: List `snapshot_provenance` and state that `ResolvedDeploy` owns an `ExitStack` released by the idempotent `close()` (also usable as a context manager), which `deploy()`/`deploy_resolved()` invoke in a `finally` to drop snapshot leases.

### F042 engineering-quality `specs/engineering-quality/low-level.md:180` accuracy
- Spec: **Pytest configuration** constrains collection to `tests/`, has strict markers/configuration/xfails, excludes `perf` by default, and recognises `slow`, `perf`, and `sandbox_strict` markers.
- Problem: The enumeration of registered pytest markers is incomplete. `pyproject.toml` registers a fourth marker, `meta`, which is load-bearing: it labels repository-health modules that are deselected from the compatibility lanes. Because `--strict-markers` is set, the marker set is a hard contract an engineer adding a test relies on, and the spec's list reads as exhaustive.
- Evidence: pyproject.toml:226-231 declares markers = ["slow: ...", "perf: ...", "sandbox_strict: ...", "meta: repo-health checks that read files rather than exercise behaviour, so their result cannot vary by interpreter; deselected from the compatibility lanes (see .github/workflows/ci.yml)"]. The same low-level.md at line 101 already calls tests/test_workflow_coverage.py "a meta-marked repository-health module", so the document contradicts itself.
- Second pass: specs/engineering-quality/low-level.md:179-181 quotes exactly as cited and lists only `slow`, `perf`, and `sandbox_strict`; pyproject.toml:226-232 registers a fourth marker `meta` under `--strict-markers`, and .github/workflows/ci.yml:281,290 deselects it with `-m "not perf and not meta"`. The same spec at line 101 calls tests/test_workflow_coverage.py "a meta-marked repository-health module", so the document is internally inconsistent. P2 is right: a stale/incomplete factual enumeration, not a persisted-work or contract contradiction.
- Fix: Add `meta` to the recognised-marker list and state that meta-marked repository-health modules are deselected from the Python compatibility lanes in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (Control flow item 4).

### F178 expression-parsing `specs/expression-parsing/high-level.md:123` accuracy
- Spec: division/floor-division/modulo by zero yield `±inf`/`nan` (float) or `null` (int) instead of raising
- Problem: The int/float split is stated for all three operators, but true division by zero returns ±inf (or nan) for integer operands too, because Polars' true division promotes to float. Only `//` and `%` return null for integers. The low-level document states this correctly (line 143), so the two documents disagree.
- Evidence: src/haute/_expression_parser.py:1774-1780 `_divide_by_zero`: for `ast.Div` it returns `math.nan` when left == 0 else `math.copysign(math.inf, left)` with no int check; the int→None branches are guarded by `left_is_float` only for `FloorDiv` (1781-1786) and `Mod` (1787-1789).
- Second pass: specs/expression-parsing/high-level.md:123 lumps all three operators ("division/floor-division/modulo by zero yield `±inf`/`nan` (float) or `null` (int)"), but src/haute/_expression_parser.py:1774-1780 returns nan/±inf for `ast.Div` with no int check — its own docstring says "Polars' true-division always promotes to float, so int / 0 is inf too" — while the `None` branches are guarded by `left_is_float` only for FloorDiv (1781-1786) and Mod (1787-1789). low-level.md:142-144 states this correctly, so the two documents disagree; contradiction between documents is P2 rather than the P3 assigned.
- Fix: Reword to "true division by zero yields ±inf/nan for both int and float operands (Polars promotes to float); integer `//` and `%` by zero yield null, float `%` yields nan."

### F053 expression-parsing `specs/expression-parsing/high-level.md:163` governance
- Spec: `evaluate_expression` used to fall back to the pipeline's actually-observed output value (`row_values.get(target_column)`) whenever the evaluator raised. That fallback was removed
- Problem: R1 forbids resolved history in a spec; this paragraph (and its low-level twin) describes a removed prior behaviour rather than describing the code as it is. The same violation appears in low-level.md lines 238-241 ("The removed prior behaviour — silently falling back to `row_values.get(target_column)` — is documented in the module as deliberately deleted").
- Evidence: src/haute/_expression_parser.py:1420-1436 `evaluate_expression` simply delegates to `_evaluate_expression_impl`; there is no fallback code path, only the docstring's historical note. The present-tense fact (exceptions propagate to the enrichment caller) is already stated in the Failure model.
- Second pass: specs/expression-parsing/high-level.md:163-164 reads verbatim "`evaluate_expression` used to fall back ... That fallback was removed", and specs/expression-parsing/low-level.md:238-241 repeats "The removed prior behaviour — silently falling back to `row_values.get(target_column)`". Both narrate a superseded state as such, which R1 forbids; src/haute/_expression_parser.py:1420-1436 shows `evaluate_expression` simply delegating to `_evaluate_expression_impl` with the history only in its docstring.
- Fix: Rewrite as present-tense rationale: "An evaluator exception propagates to the enrichment caller rather than being replaced by `row_values.get(target_column)`, because a laundered result looks self-consistent with the trace and hides evaluator divergence." Delete the removal narrative in both documents.

### F055 frontend-assistant-ui `specs/frontend-assistant-ui/high-level.md:57` accuracy
- Spec: Invalid or missing egress policy disables sending with the field-specific backend migration reason.
- Problem: Only a *missing* egress table becomes a readiness reason the composer renders. An *invalid* egress policy raises at the backend edge and returns HTTP 400 from the status endpoint, so the frontend sets `status = "error"` and renders the panel's error-with-retry state plus the generic "Assistant status could not be loaded" gate message — not a field-specific reason. The phrase "migration reason" also has no counterpart in the code.
- Evidence: src/haute/assistant/_config.py:465-471 returns the missing-table text as a readiness reason, while _validate_egress (lines 332-378) raises ConfigError for invalid values; src/haute/routes/assistant.py:133-136 turns that HauteError into HTTPException(400); frontend/src/stores/useAssistantStore.ts:277-284 maps any status fetch rejection to `status: "error"`, and line 86 renders "Assistant status could not be loaded. Try again."
- Second pass: specs/frontend-assistant-ui/high-level.md:56-57 reads "Invalid or missing egress policy disables sending with the field-specific backend migration reason." Only the missing case yields a readiness reason (src/haute/assistant/_config.py:465-471 returns the "Missing required [assistant].egress table…" string, which is not field-specific); every invalid value raises ConfigError in _validate_egress (_config.py:332-378), which src/haute/routes/assistant.py:133-136 turns into HTTPException(400), so frontend/src/stores/useAssistantStore.ts:277-284 sets status:"error" and line 86 renders the generic "Assistant status could not be loaded. Try again." No occurrence of "migration" exists anywhere in the code or backend messages.
- Fix: Split the two cases: a missing `[assistant].egress` table renders as an ordinary unconfigured reason in the composer; an invalid egress policy fails the status fetch and renders the panel's error state with retry. Drop "migration".

### F057 frontend-assistant-ui `specs/frontend-assistant-ui/low-level.md:99` accuracy
- Spec: Guards, in order: `turnStatus === "idle"`; `status.configured` and `status.mutations_enabled`; the current document is synchronized and not read-only; not inside a submodel
- Problem: The stated guard order does not match the implementation. The single gate helper checks read-only LAST, after the submodel and dirty-canvas checks, so with both a dirty canvas and a degraded/read-only document the analyst is told to save or discard canvas changes rather than shown the recovery reason.
- Evidence: frontend/src/stores/useAssistantStore.ts:85-92 `assistantSendDisabledReason` evaluates, in order: status "unknown", status "error", `!status.configured`, `!status.mutations_enabled`, `isInsideSubmodel`, `dirty`, then `readOnly`. `sendMessage` (line 364) calls the same helper, so the imperative order is identical.
- Second pass: specs/frontend-assistant-ui/low-level.md:99-101 states the guards "in order" as idle; configured/mutations_enabled; synchronized and not read-only; not inside a submodel. frontend/src/stores/useAssistantStore.ts:78-92 (assistantSendDisabledReason) evaluates status "unknown", status "error", !configured, !mutations_enabled, isInsideSubmodel, dirty, then readOnly last, and sendMessage uses the same helper — so both the submodel/read-only ordering and the two status branches are misstated.
- Fix: Restate the order as it is: idle turn; unknown/error status; configured; mutations_enabled; not inside a submodel; canvas not dirty; document not read-only — and note the two unknown/error status branches that also block sending.

### F061 frontend-git-ui `specs/frontend-git-ui/low-level.md:256` stale (partial)

"peek" : "working"}`, remounting it. The cited line is wrong: the quoted sentence is at specs/frontend-git-ui/low-level.md:253-255; line 256 is the separate "Byte-identical revalidation" bullet.
- Fix: Restate the invariant as: `refresh()` never dispatches an expansion action, and `expanded` returns to `{}` only because replacing the keyed branch scope re-runs `initialState()`.

### F088 frontend-shared `specs/frontend-shared/low-level.md:135` accuracy
- Spec: **`NodeResultsState`**: the store's full shape — six job/result record pairs (`{previews, solveResults+solveJobs, trainResults+trainJobs, exploreResults+exploreJobs}`), a `columnCache` keyed `"nodeId"` or `"nodeId:source"`, and a `pinnedPreviewNodeId` that is exempted from LRU eviction across all four result caches.

- Second pass: low-level.md:135-141 calls `NodeResultsState` the "full shape" yet omits `pivotResults`/`pivotJobs` and `pivotStartClaims`, which frontend/src/stores/useNodeResultsStore.ts:752-756 declares, and says pinning is exempt "across all four result caches" while `trimExplorePivotCache` (:701-726) also protects entries whose `entry.nodeId === pinnedNodeId`, making five. The "six ... pairs" count matches nothing in the listed group either.

### F084 frontend-shared `specs/frontend-shared/high-level.md:167` accuracy
- Spec: Each result category (`previews`, `solveResults`, `trainResults`, `exploreResults`) is bounded to a fixed entry count and evicted by least-recently-touched, except the currently pinned/open node's entry, which survives eviction pressure.
- Problem: The pivot result cache is a fifth bounded, LRU-evicted, pin-protected category and is missing from the enumeration, while high-level.md:39-41 says pivot results "retain successful matrices across pane unmounts" without mentioning any bound — a reader could conclude pivot matrices are retained indefinitely.
- Evidence: frontend/src/stores/useNodeResultsStore.ts:42 `export const MAX_CACHED_EXPLORE_PIVOT_RESULTS = 32`; `trimExplorePivotCache` (lines 701-720) prunes the recency map, computes `evictCount = keys.length - MAX_CACHED_EXPLORE_PIVOT_RESULTS`, and orders unpinned keys before keys whose `entry.nodeId === pinnedNodeId`.
- Second pass: high-level.md:167-171 enumerates only `previews`, `solveResults`, `trainResults`, `exploreResults` as bounded LRU categories, but frontend/src/stores/useNodeResultsStore.ts:42 defines `MAX_CACHED_EXPLORE_PIVOT_RESULTS = 32` and `trimExplorePivotCache` (lines 701-726) evicts pivot entries by recency with pinned-node keys ordered last. high-level.md:39-41 describes pivot matrices as retained across unmounts with no bound mentioned.
- Fix: Add `pivotResults` (bound 32, pinned Explore node's pivots protected) to the bounded-category list.

### F085 frontend-shared `specs/frontend-shared/high-level.md:296` governance
- Spec: `CachedExploreResult` already tracked all three; solve/train results and `useStaleConfigEstimate`'s cached-result contract used to compare `configHash` alone, which let a cached solve/train/estimate result from one data source silently read as current after switching to another — same config hash, wrong source's data. Both were widened to the same three-field key in one change rather than leaving two different staleness definitions in the codebase.
- Problem: R1 violation: this is resolved change history ("used to compare", "were widened ... in one change") rather than a present-tense description of the code as it is. The rationale can be stated without narrating the migration.
- Evidence: The current code has only the three-field key: frontend/src/hooks/useStaleConfigEstimate.ts takes the required `context: {source, structuralVersion}` and `CachedSolveResult`/`CachedTrainResult` carry `configHash`/`source`/`structuralVersion` (frontend/src/stores/useNodeResultsStore.ts). No `configHash`-only comparison remains, so the described prior state is not current behaviour.
- Second pass: high-level.md:294-303 reads "...used to compare `configHash` alone, which let a cached ... Both were widened to the same three-field key in one change" — narration of a superseded state and a past change, which the governance rule forbids; the current code (frontend/src/hooks/useStaleConfigEstimate.ts's required `context: {source, structuralVersion}`) has only the three-field key.
- Fix: Rewrite as present-tense rationale: the staleness key is configHash + source + structuralVersion because a matching config hash under a different data source would otherwise read as current.

### F090 frontend-trace-ui `specs/frontend-trace-ui/high-level.md:25` governance
- Spec: the backend selection is displayed as separate metadata until the wire contract carries backend-authored branch structure.

Nothing in the code represents the future wire contract.

The present-tense half is accurate: frontend/src/trace/CalculationHero.tsx:451-465 parses branches client-side with parseBranches() and renders "Selected branch: {backendTakenBranchIndex}" as a separate metadata line.

### F091 frontend-trace-ui `specs/frontend-trace-ui/low-level.md:37` missing
- Spec: `frontend/src/hooks/useTracing.ts` captures graph `structuralVersion`, active source, row limit, streaming chunk size, target, row, column, and clicked values with each request.
- Problem: Control flow and the Failure model omit the document-identity gate that also governs every trace request. A trace is silently not started at all when the document lacks execute capability or the graph is unsynchronised, and an in-flight result is silently discarded back to `idle` (no alert) when document identity changes. This is a silent-fallback path the Failure model claims does not exist for request failures.
- Evidence: frontend/src/hooks/useTracing.ts:320-323 returns early from `startTrace` when `documentSnapshot.capabilities?.can_execute !== true || !documentSnapshot.graphSynchronized`; :325-331 defines `documentStillCurrent()` over sourceFile/sourceRevision/loadStatus/graphSynchronized/can_execute; :382-393 and :404-412 clear result, cell and state to `{ status: "idle" }` when that guard fails, in both the resolve and reject paths.
- Second pass: frontend/src/hooks/useTracing.ts:319-323 returns from startTrace with no state change when capabilities?.can_execute !== true || !graphSynchronized; :325-331 defines documentStillCurrent() over sourceFile/sourceRevision/loadStatus/graphSynchronized/can_execute, and both the resolve (:379-392) and reject (:400-412) paths clear result/cell and set { status: "idle" } with no alert. Neither Control flow step 1 (low-level.md:37-42) nor either Failure model (high-level.md:56-62, low-level.md:92-99) mentions this precondition or the silent mid-flight discard, while the specs assert request problems surface as persistent visible alerts.
- Fix: Add the document-status precondition and the mid-flight identity discard to Control flow step 1, and state in the Failure model that these paths clear the panel silently rather than raising an alert.

### F092 frontend-trace-ui `specs/frontend-trace-ui/low-level.md:98` accuracy (partial)
- Spec: Pure parsers intentionally throw on invalid required numerical contracts instead of silently showing a plausible-but-wrong calculation.
- Problem: No runtime module in this component throws. Every helper in `frontend/src/trace/` and `frontend/src/panels/trace/` returns null/guarded values instead of raising, so an engineer relying on a throwing parser contract (e.g. wrapping a call in try/catch, or expecting a loud failure on a malformed numeric field) would be misled about the actual error surface.
- Evidence: `grep -rn "throw" frontend/src/trace/ frontend/src/panels/trace/ frontend/src/panels/TracePanel.tsx --include=*.ts --include=*.tsx` (excluding __tests__) returns zero matches. The only throw-related assertion in the component's tests is frontend/src/trace/__tests__/traceExport.test.ts:143, which asserts the caller-supplied clipboard writer's rejection propagates, not a parser contract.
- Second pass: The sentence at specs/frontend-trace-ui/low-level.md:98 is unsupported — grep for "throw" across frontend/src/trace/, frontend/src/panels/trace/ and frontend/src/panels/TracePanel.tsx (excluding __tests__) returns zero matches, so no parser throws on an invalid numerical contract. But the finding's absolute claim that "no runtime module in this component throws" is wrong: frontend/src/hooks/useTracing.ts:459, :465 and :478 (a module-map file) throw Error on malformed submodel identity config — non-numerical, so the spec claim is still inaccurate, just for a narrower reason than stated.
- Fix: Replace the sentence with the shipped behaviour: helper type guards (e.g. `asBandingDetail`, `asModelScoreDetail`, the `*Helpers` extractors) return null/absent markers for malformed optional fields and the renderers show an explicit alert or omission, rather than throwing.

- Spec: `src/haute/routes/git.py` | FastAPI router at `/api/git`. One `def` (sync) handler per endpoint, each a thin `try/except` around a single Git-domain call; converts the domain layer's typed exceptions to HTTP responses via `_handle_git_error`.
- Problem: Six handlers in this module are not thin wrappers around a Git-domain call and do not route errors through `_handle_git_error`; one documented route also performs a non-git side effect. But the evidence overstates the error-handling claim: only git_bind_storage (617-622) and git_fork_storage (668-673) bypass _handle_git_error; bind/ack (645), upstream/check (748), upstream/pull (778) and retry (796) all call it. Shared ownership is already recorded at specs/ownership.toml:332-336, so that part is not a defect, only supporting context.
- Fix: Qualify the Module-map line ("each git route is a thin try/except …; the storage endpoints hosted in this file are owned by [hosted-project-storage]\(../hosted-project-storage/low-level.md)") and add one sentence under HTTP contracts pointing at that component for the `/api/git/storage/*` rows and the milestone route's post-commit push enqueue.

### F095 hosted-databricks-app `specs/hosted-databricks-app/high-level.md:39` accuracy
- Spec: For HTTP and WebSocket traffic, the boundary records a non-empty `X-Forwarded-Email` on the ASGI scope
- Problem: The boundary applies no non-emptiness test. Any present `X-Forwarded-Email` header, including one with an empty or whitespace value, is decoded and written to the scope key, so a blank identity is recorded as a user. The low-level invariant at line 32 ("absent rather than empty when the proxy sends no identity, so consumers cannot mistake 'no identity' for a blank user") states the same guarantee and is equally contradicted; consumers such as `src/haute/routes/git.py:614` (`bound_by`) and `src/haute/server.py:518` (log `user`) receive the empty string.
- Evidence: src/haute/hosted.py:112-121 — the loop sets `forwarded_user = value.decode("latin-1")` for any header named `x-forwarded-email`, and the scope key is written under `if forwarded_user is not None`, so `b""` yields `scope[FORWARDED_USER_SCOPE_KEY] == ""`. tests/test_hosted.py:124-133 covers only the populated header and the wholly absent header; no empty-value case exists.
- Second pass: specs/hosted-databricks-app/high-level.md:39-40 reads "the boundary records a non-empty `X-Forwarded-Email` on the ASGI scope" and low-level.md:32 asserts the key is "absent rather than empty". src/haute/hosted.py:112-121 records `forwarded_user = value.decode("latin-1")` for any header named x-forwarded-email and writes the scope key under `if forwarded_user is not None`, so a present-but-empty header yields "". Consumers src/haute/routes/git.py:614 (bound_by) and 668 (forked_by) and src/haute/server.py:518 then persist/log a blank identity; tests/test_hosted.py has no empty-value case (only "someone@example.com" at line 35).
- Fix: Either state the shipped behaviour ("records the `X-Forwarded-Email` value when the header is present") in both documents, or, if the emptiness guard is the intended contract, add the guard in `src/haute/hosted.py` plus a test and keep the current wording.

### F096 hosted-databricks-app `specs/hosted-databricks-app/high-level.md:91` accuracy
- Spec: A partial or whitespace-only hosted environment fails at startup and names the missing contract variables without exposing their values.
- Problem: A whitespace-only environment does not fail at startup. Values are stripped before the presence test, so when all three variables hold only whitespace nothing is present and detection returns `None` — the ordinary local posture — rather than raising. Only a genuinely partial set raises.
- Evidence: src/haute/hosted.py:74-84 — `values = {name: (env.get(name) or "").strip() ...}`; `if not present: return None` precedes the missing-variable `RuntimeError`. tests/test_hosted.py:78-79 `test_whitespace_values_count_as_absent` asserts `databricks_app_environment(environ=dict.fromkeys(_CONTRACT, "  ")) is None`.
- Second pass: specs/hosted-databricks-app/high-level.md:91 says "A partial or whitespace-only hosted environment fails at startup". src/haute/hosted.py:74-77 strips values before the presence test and returns None when nothing is present, so an all-whitespace contract is the ordinary local posture, not a startup failure; only a set that is partial after stripping reaches the RuntimeError at line 79.
- Fix: Reword to: whitespace-only values count as absent, so an all-whitespace environment is the local posture; a set that is partial after stripping fails at startup naming the missing variables.

- Evidence: src/haute/_json_shred/_cache.py:325-331 in `prepare_per_port_cache`: `table_specs = _shred._emitting_table_specs(...)`, `fingerprint = _shred._v2_fingerprint(...)`, `data_file_sig = _source_proof._data_file_signature(dp, ...)` all run first, then `if _cache_is_valid_under_external_lock(cd, v2_config, data_path=dp, data_file_signature=data_file_sig):` returns the no-op summary. Also, the lock is taken as `_publication.per_port_cache_publication_lock(cd)` (src/haute/_json_shred/_cache.py:538) and the staging path is minted at :539 before `prepare_per_port_cache` runs, whereas the spec places staging creation at step 6.
- Second pass: The ordering claim holds: src/haute/_json_shred/_cache.py:326-335 computes `_emitting_table_specs`, `_v2_fingerprint` and `_data_file_signature` before the `_cache_is_valid_under_external_lock` no-op trapdoor at :331-335, inverting spec steps 3-5 (and step 1's `validate_v2_schema` actually runs inside `_emitting_table_specs`, src/haute/_json_shred/_shred.py:311). The lock sub-claim is wrong, though: `per_port_cache_publication_lock` (src/haute/_json_shred/_publication.py:411-413) is a thin wrapper over `_build_lock_for`, so spec step 2 is accurate, and the spec's step-6 statement that staging "precedes the shred" is also true even though it is minted earlier at _cache.py:540.
- Fix: Reorder steps 3-6 to: acquire `per_port_cache_publication_lock`; mint the unique staging path; build the table specs, fingerprint and data-file signature; then the no-op trapdoor against the already-computed signature; then create the staging directory and shred.

### F203 json-shredding `specs/json-shredding/high-level.md:233` governance (partial)
- Spec: After that complete hash, Haute may atomically upgrade a live legacy manifest with the new revision-bound proof only when the manifest's recorded size and SHA-256 match the freshly observed source.
- Problem: R1 states Haute is prerelease and each boundary accepts exactly its canonical format; describing an old-manifest-format upgrade/compatibility path (repeated at low-level.md:653 "each live legacy v2 manifest") is the kind of compatibility path the rule forbids in a spec, and it also documents an old on-disk format alongside the current one.
- Evidence: src/haute/_json_shred/_source_proof.py:450 reads `native_revision_proof_sha256` from a recorded manifest and falls through to a full hash when it is missing/mismatched; `rebind_persisted_proofs` (src/haute/_json_shred/_cache.py:329) gates the rewrite. The behaviour exists, but it is an accommodation for manifests written without the proof field — an old-format path.
- Second pass: The word "legacy" at high-level.md:233 and low-level.md:653 is inaccurate, but not for the reason given: src/haute/_json_shred/_source_proof.py:504-511 documents `_rebind_persisted_source_proofs` as "the fresh-host/first-observation path, not a version shim" — it rebinds a current-format manifest whose content signature matches but whose recorded native revision differs (another volume, or a host that could not observe a revision), and :528-531 skips any manifest whose `data_file` payload already equals the fresh one. So the finding's premise of an old-manifest-format compatibility path is refuted; what remains is that the spec mislabels a live current-format rebind as a "legacy manifest" upgrade.
- Fix: Either drop the manifest-upgrade path from the code and spec (prerelease: manifests without the proof field simply fail validity and are rebuilt), or, if it is retained deliberately, describe it without the word "legacy" as a plain rule about manifests whose proof field is absent, and record the decision as a roadmap package.

- Fix: Add a bullet to `## Error handling` naming `haute._json_shred._cache.SourceChangedDuringCacheBuildError` (a `RuntimeError` subclass) — raised when the recorded source signature no longer matches after the shred and before publication — and add the corresponding sentence to the high-level Failure model with the route status it produces.

### F115 optimiser `specs/optimiser/low-level.md:246` accuracy
- Spec: `frontier_auto_range_status` enforces the job's timeout lazily, on poll — there is no dedicated timeout-watcher thread for auto-range jobs, unlike solves (see below).
- Problem: The clause "unlike solves" asserts that solve jobs have a dedicated timeout-watcher thread. They do not: solve timeouts are enforced exactly the same way, lazily on poll of the status route. An engineer reading this would look for (or preserve) a watcher thread that does not exist.
- Evidence: src/haute/routes/optimiser.py:1400-1418 `solve_status` reads `job.get("timeout")` and `start`, and only when `(time.monotonic() - start) > timeout` calls `_solve_service.timeout_solve(job_id, ...)`. A repository-wide grep of `src/haute/routes/_optimiser_service.py` for `Timer(`, `watcher`, `_watch_timeout` returns no matches, so no timeout-watcher thread exists for solves either. The same file's own line 301 correctly describes the frontier-status route as using the "no dedicated timeout-watcher thread" pattern.
- Second pass: specs/optimiser/low-level.md:246-247 reads exactly as quoted ("...no dedicated timeout-watcher thread for auto-range jobs, unlike solves (see below)."). src/haute/routes/optimiser.py:1399-1418 shows `solve_status` enforcing the timeout lazily on poll (`if job.get("status") == "running"` ... `(time.monotonic() - start) > timeout` -> `_solve_service.timeout_solve`), identical in shape to the frontier path at optimiser.py:1841-1861; a grep for `Timer(`/`watcher`/`Thread(` in optimiser.py and _optimiser_service.py finds only the four worker-spawn threads (_setup_background, _auto_range_background, _solve_background_in_worker_context, _frontier_background), no timeout watcher. The spec also contradicts itself at line 301, which calls the lazy-poll pattern the shared one. P2: factual inaccuracy in prose, no persisted-work/route-contract impact.
- Fix: Drop "unlike solves" and state that solve, frontier-sweep, and auto-range timeouts are all enforced lazily on their respective status polls (`solve_status`, `frontier_status`, `frontier_auto_range_status`), plus the in-worker elapsed check in the auto-range loop.

### F117 pipeline-config `specs/pipeline-config/low-level.md:41` accuracy
- Spec: A live `Pipeline.submodel(file)` call only records the path in `_submodel_files`; it does not import the module or register the `Submodel` object's nodes onto the live `Pipeline`.
- Problem: `submodel()` cannot be called as `Pipeline.submodel(file)` — `definition_id`, `instance_id` and `alias` are required keyword-only arguments — and it records more than the path: it appends a `RegisteredSubmodel` occurrence record and enforces alias/instance-id uniqueness and node-name conflicts.
- Evidence: src/haute/pipeline.py:718-777 — signature `submodel(self, file, *, definition_id, instance_id, alias, instance_of=None, label=None)`; it validates all identities, raises `ValueError` on duplicate `instance_id`/`alias` or conflict with a registered node name, then appends to both `self._submodel_files` and `self._submodel_registrations` and returns `self`.
- Second pass: specs/pipeline-config/low-level.md:41-43 carries the quote. src/haute/pipeline.py:718-777 shows `submodel(self, file, *, definition_id, instance_id, alias, instance_of=None, label=None)` — the keyword args are required, identities are validated, duplicate `instance_id`/`alias` and node-name conflicts raise `ValueError`, and the call appends to both `_submodel_files` and `_submodel_registrations`, so "only records the path" is false.
- Fix: State the real signature and that the call records a `RegisteredSubmodel` occurrence (file, definition_id, instance_id, alias, instance_of, label) alongside the path, with fail-loud identity/uniqueness validation; keep the accurate "does not import or register child nodes" clause.

No chain-fallback path exists in code: src/haute/pipeline.py:452-455 `_topo_order` returns registration order when `_edges` is empty and invents nothing.
