### F118 pipeline-config `specs/pipeline-config/low-level.md:116` accuracy
- Spec: `Pipeline.to_graph()` converts the same live objects into a React-Flow-shaped plain `dict`, inferring each node's display type from `config["_node_type"]` if present, else `DATA_INPUT` for an input node, else `OUTPUT` for the last-registered node, else `POLARS`.

Both `_scaffold.py` and `cli/_init_cmd.py` are written without the `src/haute/` prefix, and `cli/_init_cmd.py` is an ambiguous partial path. - Evidence: `src/haute/cli/_init_cmd.py` is the real path; `_prune_stale_ci_files` is defined at src/haute/cli/_init_cmd.py:441 and `_CI_ARTIFACTS` at src/haute/cli/_init_cmd.py:430 — neither string occurs anywhere in src/haute/_scaffold.py. specs/cli/low-level.md:10 names `src/haute/cli/_init_cmd.py` with the fully-qualified path.
- Second pass: The path half holds: specs/pipeline-config/low-level.md:183 writes `_scaffold.py` and `cli/_init_cmd.py::handle_init` without the `src/haute/` prefix, while the Module map at line 19 uses the full `src/haute/_scaffold.py` and the real file is `src/haute/cli/_init_cmd.py`. The attribution half is overstated: lines 203-207 say `handle_init` calls `_prune_stale_ci_files(project_dir, keep=ci)` and reads `_CI_ARTIFACTS` — that matches src/haute/cli/_init_cmd.py:430,441,574 and never claims either symbol lives in `_scaffold.py`.
- Fix: Write the heading as `src/haute/_scaffold.py`, driven by `src/haute/cli/_init_cmd.py::handle_init`, and attribute `_prune_stale_ci_files`/`_CI_ARTIFACTS` explicitly to `src/haute/cli/_init_cmd.py`.

### F121 rating `specs/rating/low-level.md:19` accuracy
- Spec: Unknown pseudo-types such as `"age"`, mixed rule vocabularies such as `{key, value}`, unsupported operators, blank assignments, non-finite thresholds, and invalid breakpoint sets are rejected by the shared config validator before save/codegen as well as by runtime.
- Problem: Runtime does not reject an unknown banding pseudo-type when rules are a canonical list: `_apply_banding` branches only on `"categorical"` and `"breakpoints"` and treats every other discriminant (including `"age"`) as continuous. Only a compact dict rule map raises for an unknown type.
- Evidence: src/haute/_rating.py:110-160 `_apply_banding` — `if banding_type == "categorical": ...`, `if banding_type == "breakpoints": ...`, otherwise falls through to the continuous when/then chain with no type check; src/haute/_banding_config.py:53-64 `normalise_banding_rules` raises only for a dict rules map on an unsupported type. `SUPPORTED_BANDING_TYPES` is checked only in `validate_banding_config` (src/haute/_rating.py:296-302), which the runtime path does not call.
- Second pass: specs/rating/low-level.md:19-22 does say unknown pseudo-types etc. are rejected "by the shared config validator before save/codegen as well as by runtime". src/haute/_rating.py:112-160 `_apply_banding` branches only on "categorical" and "breakpoints" and otherwise builds the continuous when/then chain with no membership check; `SUPPORTED_BANDING_TYPES` is only consulted in `validate_banding_config` (src/haute/_rating.py:279-302), whose sole caller is src/haute/_config_validation.py:343-345. `_banding_config.normalise_banding_rules`/`_expand_rule_map` raise for an unknown type only when rules are a dict map, so an unknown discriminant with list rules runs as continuous at runtime.
- Fix: Say the unknown-pseudo-type rejection is a config-validator (save/codegen) guarantee; runtime rejects unsupported operators, non-finite thresholds, blank assignments and invalid breakpoint sets, but treats an unrecognised discriminant with list rules as continuous.

### F122 rating `specs/rating/low-level.md:47` accuracy
- Spec: `validate_banding_config(config)` validates every configured factor and returns the canonical expanded factor rows used by execution and codegen. 3. `_normalise_banding_factors(config)` delegates to that validator.
- Problem: The banding entry point does not run `validate_banding_config`. `_normalise_banding_factors` delegates to `_banding_config.normalise_banding_factors` (shape expansion only); the strict validator is invoked solely from the save-time config-validation path. The stated call chain is inverted, so a reader assumes execution/codegen enforce the strict factor checks.
- Evidence: src/haute/_rating.py:274-276 `_normalise_banding_factors` returns `normalise_banding_factors(config)` (imported from `_banding_config`); src/haute/_rating.py:389 `apply_banding_from_config` returns `_apply_banding_factors(lf, _normalise_banding_factors(resolved_config))`; src/haute/_builders.py:650 uses the same helper; the only caller of `validate_banding_config` is src/haute/_config_validation.py:343-345.
- Second pass: specs/rating/low-level.md:46-48 states step 2 is `validate_banding_config(config)` returning the canonical factors "used by execution and codegen" and step 3 delegates to that validator. src/haute/_rating.py:274-276 `_normalise_banding_factors` returns `normalise_banding_factors(config)` from `_banding_config` (shape expansion only), and src/haute/_rating.py:389 `apply_banding_from_config` calls only that helper; `validate_banding_config` is invoked exclusively from src/haute/_config_validation.py:345. The documented chain is inverted.
- Fix: State that steps 2-3 are: `_normalise_banding_factors(config)` delegates to `_banding_config.normalise_banding_factors` (sidecar expansion plus shape rejection), and that `validate_banding_config` is the save/codegen-boundary validator called from `src/haute/_config_validation.py`, with runtime enforcing usable-rule checks inside `_apply_banding`.

### F005 roadmap `specs/roadmap/engineering-quality.md:79` stale (partial)
- Spec: **Why:** F9 serialises Save requests without checking their base revision, so a valid external edit can be overwritten. F10 records self-writes by path/time, so a later external write to the same path can be suppressed.
- Problem: ENG-T02's stated cause is contradicted by the working tree: both F9 and F10 corrections are implemented, together with the frontend plumbing, the browser conflict journey and the test replacement the package itself demands. The package is still listed as an open P1 `Reverify` item asserting a live data-loss defect, so a reader is directed to reproduce and re-fix work that has shipped.
- Evidence: src/haute/schemas.py:651 `SavePipelineRequest.base_revision: RevisionToken | None`; src/haute/routes/_save_pipeline.py:67 error `code = "stale_document_revision"`, :196 `_require_base_revision`, :206-214 mismatch/absence raise, :238 called inside the save path; src/haute/routes/pipeline.py:1607 maps it to the 409 detail; src/haute/routes/_helpers.py:291-303 `mark_self_write(..., content=..., deleted=...)` records the committed SHA-256, :323-330 `is_self_write` discards a marker whose bytes no longer match; frontend/src/hooks/usePipelineAPI.ts:1194 sends `base_revision`, :1244 handles `stale_document_revision`; frontend/e2e/save-conflict.spec.ts:10 "a stale save is rejected, keeps the local edit, and succeeds after reload"; `TestConcurrentSaveAndPreview`, `test_save_during_preview_does_not_corrupt_files` and `test_save_service_is_not_thread_safe_by_design` no longer exist in tests/test_partial_failure.py (grep returns nothing), which is exactly the replacement ENG-T02 line 125-130 requires.
- Second pass: The quoted 'Why' at specs/roadmap/engineering-quality.md:79-83 is present verbatim, and the working tree contradicts both halves: src/haute/schemas.py:652 carries `base_revision: RevisionToken | None`, src/haute/routes/_save_pipeline.py:228-243 `_require_base_revision` fails closed with `StaleDocumentRevisionError` before mutation, src/haute/routes/_helpers.py:291-330 marks self-writes by committed SHA-256/deletion and discards a marker whose bytes changed, frontend/src/hooks/usePipelineAPI.ts:1195/1245 send and handle the token, and frontend/e2e/save-conflict.spec.ts:10 covers the browser journey. The finding is right that the cause is stale, but P1 overstates it: this is a roadmap file whose own index (specs/roadmap/README.md:9-11) declares `Reverify` packages to be older evidence requiring reproduction against HEAD, and stale planning prose that invents a defect hides no live defect — P2 stale content.
- Fix: Retire ENG-T02 (remove its Priorities row and section), fold the delivered revision/self-write contract into the server-api, pipeline-config and frontend-shared specifications, and keep only whatever residue (e.g. recovery-artifact participation in the precondition, disconnect/reconnect coverage) is genuinely outstanding as a narrowed package with a truthful present-tense Why.

### F126 sandbox-security `specs/sandbox-security/high-level.md:146` accuracy
- Spec: `HAUTE_DISABLE_LOCAL_SESSION_AUTH` remains an explicit local development escape hatch; the loopback/forwarded-header gate remains active.
- Problem: The variable is not only a local development escape hatch: the hosted deployment entry point sets it process-wide as its production trust model, and in that mode the forwarded-header gate is also effectively neutralised because the proxy boundary strips those headers before the middleware sees them.
- Evidence: src/haute/hosted.py:141 `os.environ[DISABLE_AUTH_ENV] = "1"` inside `create_app()`, whose docstring says "the local session gate is disabled process-wide (the documented hosted trust model)"; src/haute/hosted.py:107-123 `PlatformProxyBoundary.__call__` removes `Forwarded`/`x-forwarded-*` headers and rewrites `host` before delegating to the server app.
- Second pass: specs/sandbox-security/high-level.md:146-147 says the flag "remains an explicit local development escape hatch; the loopback/forwarded-header gate remains active." src/haute/_local_security.py:27 defines DISABLE_AUTH_ENV = "HAUTE_DISABLE_LOCAL_SESSION_AUTH", and src/haute/hosted.py:141 sets it process-wide in create_app(), whose docstring calls it "the documented hosted trust model" — a second, production consumer the spec omits. The finding's "forwarded-header gate neutralised" wording is loose (the gate still runs) but its own evidence states the accurate mechanism: headers are stripped/rewritten at hosted.py:107-122 before the middleware sees them.
- Fix: Add the second consumer: the flag is a local development escape hatch and the switch the hosted Databricks Apps entry point sets deliberately; note that in hosted mode the forwarded-header gate is satisfied by header rewriting at the proxy boundary rather than by rejection.

### F140 submodels `specs/submodels/low-level.md:214` accuracy
- Spec: `parse_pipeline_to_graph` attaches this revision to every live graph response and WebSocket refresh; a successful save reparses the committed document and returns the new revision.
- Problem: The revision clients retain and send back as `base_revision` is the raw-artifact recovery revision (pipeline_recovery_revision), not pipeline_document_revision. The submodel routes explicitly discard the parsed graph's document revision and substitute the editor document's recovery revision before compare-and-swap, so this section describes the wrong token for the route contract it documents.
- Evidence: src/haute/routes/_helpers.py:967-969 sets graph.source_revision = pipeline_document_revision(...), but src/haute/routes/submodel.py:65-72 overwrites it with editor_document.source_revision, which src/haute/_pipeline_recovery.py:2091 and :2153 compute with pipeline_recovery_revision. GET /api/pipeline returns a PipelineEditorDocument built by load_pipeline_editor_document (src/haute/routes/pipeline.py:619-630), i.e. the recovery revision; save compares document.source_revision at src/haute/routes/pipeline.py:1605.
- Second pass: specs/submodels/low-level.md:214 quoted verbatim, but no live editor response uses parse_pipeline_to_graph's document revision: GET /pipeline/{name} returns load_pipeline_editor_document (src/haute/routes/pipeline.py:618-630) whose source_revision comes from pipeline_recovery_revision (src/haute/_pipeline_recovery.py:2091), save returns committed_document.source_revision (src/haute/routes/_save_pipeline.py:402,431), and src/haute/routes/submodel.py:72-73 explicitly overwrites the parsed graph's revision with editor_document.source_revision. The finding slightly overstates only in implying the document is uniformly wrong — low-level.md:9 and :303-306 do describe the raw-artifact revision correctly — but the quoted sentence itself is inaccurate on both halves.
- Fix: State that live editor documents and WebSocket refreshes carry the recovery revision, and add a Control flow subsection for `src/haute/_pipeline_revision.py::pipeline_recovery_revision` describing the role-qualified raw-artifact manifest with present/missing/unreadable states.

### F144 tracing `specs/tracing/low-level.md:97` accuracy
- Spec: **`source_frames_of`** (`trace.py`, built in `execute_trace`; threaded through `_correlate_rows_posthoc` and `enrich_steps`) — `dict[tuple[source_id, target_id], list[str | None]]`
- Problem: `source_frames_of` is passed only to `enrich_steps`. The correlation layer receives a different structure, `edge_metadata`, mapping the pair to `(sourceHandle, targetHandle)` tuples per edge — the target handle is load-bearing there (it selects the edge-join base/join role). The claim is repeated in the `_resolve_multi_frame_parent` section (line 237: "`handles` set to `source_frames_of[(parent_id, child_id)]`") and in the `_correlate_rows_posthoc` section (line 269: "`handles=source_frames_of.get((nid, resolved_child_id))`"); the actual parameter is `edges`.
- Evidence: src/haute/trace.py:657-668 calls `_correlate_rows_posthoc(..., edge_metadata=edge_metadata, ...)` and :706-715 calls `_enrich_steps(..., source_frames_of=source_frames_of, incoming_edges_of=incoming_edges_of)`. src/haute/_trace_correlation.py:1423 declares `edge_metadata: Mapping[tuple[str,str], Sequence[tuple[str|None,str|None]]]`; :1217 `_resolve_multi_frame_parent(..., edges: Sequence[tuple[str|None,str|None]] | None, ...)`; :1233 `edges=(edge_metadata or {}).get((nid, resolved_child_id))`.
- Second pass: src/haute/trace.py:657-668 passes `edge_metadata=edge_metadata` to `_correlate_rows_posthoc` and only :706-715 passes `source_frames_of=` (to `_enrich_steps`); trace.py:591-599 shows `source_frames_of` is a handle-only projection of `edge_metadata`, and _trace_correlation.py:1217/1233 use the `edges`/(sourceHandle,targetHandle) structure, so low-level.md:97-99, :237 and :269-270 misname the correlation-side parameter and drop the load-bearing target handle.
- Fix: Document `edge_metadata` (pair -> list of `(sourceHandle, targetHandle)` per edge) as the correlation-side structure and `source_frames_of` as the enrichment-side projection, and rename `handles` to `edges` in the `_resolve_multi_frame_parent` and `_correlate_rows_posthoc` sections.

### F010 tracing `specs/tracing/low-level.md:242` accuracy (partial)
- Spec: Deduplicate `handles` into an ordered set of non-`None` handles, then keep only the frames that exist in the parent's `dict` and are non-empty as `candidates`.
- Problem: `_resolve_multi_frame_parent` does not deduplicate handles: it appends one candidate per physical edge. The spec's stated "common case: a single edge, or several edges all naming the same frame" therefore does not take the single-candidate delegation path in code — two edges naming the same frame produce two identical candidates, both match with identical `match_width`, and the tie-break falls through to the `ambiguous_source_frame` branch, returning `(None, -1)` (an unresolved step / omission) instead of the correlated row the spec promises.
- Evidence: src/haute/_trace_correlation.py:1243-1250 — `for handle, target_role in edges or (): ... candidates.append(_FrameCandidate(handle, target_role, frame))` with no dedup; the only deduplicated structure is `source_handles` (line 1241), used solely for the diagnostic payload. Lines 1272 (`if len(candidates) == 1`) and 1331-1358 (width tie -> `ambiguous_source_frame`, return `None, -1`) show the divergent outcome.
- Second pass: specs/tracing/low-level.md:241-243 does say "Deduplicate `handles` into an ordered set", and src/haute/_trace_correlation.py:1241-1250 builds one `_FrameCandidate` per edge with no dedup (only `source_handles` at :1241 is a set, used for diagnostics), so the accuracy defect is real. The claimed consequence is overstated/conditional: duplicate candidates carry distinct `target_role` values (:1247), and `_match_parent_row` behaves differently per role (`_allows_relaxed_parent_match`), so the `ambiguous_source_frame` return of `(None, -1)` (:1338-1358) only follows when both role and match width coincide; trace output is a read-only diagnostic view, so P2 rather than P1.
- Fix: Either describe the shipped behaviour (one candidate per edge, duplicates allowed, resolved by the traced-column/width/tie rules) or, if dedup is intended, note it as a defect with a roadmap package. Also state that duplicate-handle edges are compared independently.

## P3 findings

### F151 assistant `specs/assistant/low-level.md:28` consistency
- Spec: The only provider-visible mutation tools are `dry_run_graph_edits` and `apply_graph_plan`: the model must pass the exact returned plan hash and cannot resend operations at apply time.
- Problem: The Control flow section later describes `plan_recipe` and `dry_run_recipe_plan` as equally provider-visible tools on the mutation path, and the code groups `dry_run_recipe_plan` with `dry_run_graph_edits`. An engineer reading the Module map alone would believe the mutation tool surface is exactly two operations.
- Evidence: `src/haute/assistant/_catalog.py:1153` treats `{"dry_run_graph_edits", "dry_run_recipe_plan"}` identically, and line 1141 marks only `apply_graph_plan` as `mutation`. `src/haute/assistant/_loop.py:51` defines `_DRY_RUN_TOOLS = frozenset({"dry_run_graph_edits", "dry_run_recipe_plan"})`.
- Second pass: specs/assistant/low-level.md:28 asserts the provider-visible mutation tools are "exactly" `dry_run_graph_edits` and `apply_graph_plan`, but src/haute/assistant/_catalog.py:1141 marks only `apply_graph_plan` as mutation and :1153 treats `{"dry_run_graph_edits", "dry_run_recipe_plan"}` identically (mirrored by `_DRY_RUN_TOOLS` at src/haute/assistant/_loop.py:51); low-level.md:224-227 presents `plan_recipe`/`dry_run_recipe_plan` as an equally provider-facing planning path.
- Fix: Say `apply_graph_plan` is the only tool that writes, and that the mutation path is `plan_recipe`/`dry_run_recipe_plan` or `dry_run_graph_edits` followed by `apply_graph_plan` with the exact returned hash.

### F153 background-jobs `specs/background-jobs/low-level.md:157` accuracy
- Spec: 1. Validate the destination, optimistic-lock state, and payload fields before entering the store. ... Any other value or destination raises `ValueError`, as does any attempt for `fields` to supply a store-owned lifecycle key.
- Problem: `JobLifecycle.transition` itself validates only `to` and `expected_status`; the lifecycle-key rejection for `fields` happens inside `JobStore.transition_terminal`, i.e. after entering the store (though still before any mutation). The step as written misplaces the guard in the facade.
- Evidence: src/haute/routes/_job_lifecycle.py:87-101 validates `to not in TERMINAL_REASONS` and `expected_status`, then delegates; the `fields` check is `self._validate_payload_fields(payload)` at src/haute/routes/_job_store.py:567 inside `transition_terminal`.
- Second pass: src/haute/routes/_job_lifecycle.py:87-101 validates only `to` and `expected_status` before delegating; the payload-field rejection is `self._validate_payload_fields(payload)` at src/haute/routes/_job_store.py:622 inside transition_terminal, so low-level.md:156-160's "before entering the store" misplaces that guard (it does still precede any mutation, which is why P3, not higher).
- Fix: Say the facade validates destination and expected status, and that the store validates payload fields before any record mutation.

- Spec: `touch_heavy_objects(job_id, required_keys=...)` lets a consumer that just successfully read a completed job's heavy state extend its retention window

- Fix: Name `_DEFAULT_HEAVY_OBJECT_KEYS` in the Key-types heavy-object bullet and state that it, not `_HEAVY_OBJECT_KEYS`, is the `touch_heavy_objects` default.

### F155 background-jobs `specs/background-jobs/low-level.md:297` missing
- Spec: ### `CancellableJobRegistry`

- `register_latest(key, job_id, execution_token=None)` ... - `cancel(job_id, reason="cancelled")` ... - `is_cancelled` / `cancellation_reason` ... - `release(job_id)`
- Problem: The enumeration of the registry's public surface omits `latest_publication(job_id)`, a public context manager that holds the registry lock across a caller's final publication so the latest-owner check and the commit are indivisible relative to `register_latest`/`cancel`. This is a concurrency contract a consumer must know about (and it holds the registry `RLock` for the duration of the caller's block).
- Evidence: src/haute/routes/_background_jobs.py:125-140 `@contextmanager def latest_publication(self, job_id) -> Iterator[bool]` yields `token is not None and not token.cancelled and self._latest_by_key.get(token.key) == job_id` while holding `self._lock`. Neither spec document mentions the name.
- Second pass: src/haute/routes/_background_jobs.py:125-140 defines the public `latest_publication` context manager that yields the latest-owner verdict while holding the registry RLock; grep across specs/ finds it only in specs/explore-eda/low-level.md:359, never in either background-jobs document, whose CancellableJobRegistry surface list (low-level.md:297+) omits it.
- Fix: Add a bullet documenting `latest_publication` and its lock-held-across-caller-block requirement.

The ambiguity is real, but the claimed collision is weaker than stated: line 15 says "PEP 561 marker", a distinct phrase, and line 33 already establishes `src/haute/static/` as generated, so a careful reader is unlikely to read this as py.typed; P3 is right.

### F157 build-and-distribution `specs/build-and-distribution/low-level.md:150` missing (partial)
- Spec: `_npm()` raises `RuntimeError` if npm is not on PATH and the known Windows location is unavailable.
- Problem: The spec documents npm resolution but never mentions the parallel Node resolution: the hook also augments the subprocess environment with the Windows Node.js directory when `node` is absent from PATH. That behaviour affects whether a build succeeds on a machine where Node is installed but not on PATH, and is invisible in both documents.
- Evidence: hatch_build.py:597-608 `_node_env()` returns a copy of os.environ with `C:\Program Files\nodejs` prepended to PATH when `shutil.which("node")` is falsy on win32; hatch_build.py:620 passes it as `env=` to every subprocess.
- Second pass: hatch_build.py:597-608 `_node_env()` copies os.environ and prepends `C:\Program Files\nodejs` when `shutil.which("node")` is falsy on win32, and hatch_build.py:620 passes `env=self._node_env()` to every subprocess; the spec's Error-handling bullet (line 150-154) documents only the parallel `_npm()` Windows fallback. The omission is genuine and the asymmetry with the documented npm fallback is a real gap, but it is an omitted platform-convenience detail rather than a spec statement the code contradicts, so the finding slightly overstates it as an inaccuracy; P3 stands.
- Fix: Add one sentence noting that `_run()` supplies an environment from `FrontendBuildHook._node_env()`, which prepends the known Windows Node.js directory when `node` is not on PATH.

### F159 caching `specs/caching/low-level.md:121` accuracy
- Spec: Status validates the same schema and storage metadata; delete clears the cache.
- Problem: Delete does not clear "the cache": it removes only the volatile `working/` layer and deliberately leaves the durable `committed/` layer, which continues to serve runs. Given that the adjacent invariant makes the two-layer distinction load-bearing for status, describing delete as clearing the cache would mislead a reader into thinking a delete guarantees a rebuild.
- Evidence: src/haute/routes/json_cache.py:865-872 — the `@router.delete("")` docstring and body: "Delete the volatile (working/) cache layer for a structured input file. Dual-cache semantics: delete operates on the working/ layer only. The durable committed/ layer is untouched and remains the source of ...".
- Second pass: specs/caching/low-level.md:121 says "delete clears the cache", but src/haute/routes/json_cache.py:865-880 (`@router.delete("")`) documents and implements working/-layer-only deletion: "The durable committed/ layer is untouched and remains the source of truth...", calling `clear_json_cache(data_path)`. Misleading given the adjacent two-layer status invariant, but no code contradiction of a persisted-work claim — P3 is right.
- Fix: Say that delete removes the `working/` layer only and leaves `committed/` intact, and mirror that in the high-level behaviour sentence listing the build endpoints.

The clause is genuinely past-tense and implies a pre-change refusal, but it reads as counterfactual design rationale for why per-table sizing exists ("Without this, ... was refused") rather than a narration of a superseded state as such, which the strict reading permits as rationale; the finding overstates it at P2 and P3 is the fair grade.

Only the trailing clause "Reading the result inside the child once cost `interpolate` roughly 60 MiB and made a streaming operator look like a boundary." is verbatim and is genuine past-state narration, so a much narrower P3 issue stands.

### F189 frontend-modelling-optimiser-ui `specs/frontend-modelling-optimiser-ui/high-level.md:29` accuracy (partial)
- Spec: Modelling preview exposes only result-backed tabs (summary, coefficients/relativities, loss, lift, residuals, feature importance, AVE and PDP) and resets selection when the result changes.
- Problem: Summary is not result-backed in the same sense as the other tabs: it is unconditionally available whenever a result exists and is also the fallback when the remembered tab is unavailable. The blanket "only result-backed tabs" claim would mislead an engineer adding a tab into thinking Summary has a data predicate.
- Evidence: frontend/src/panels/ModellingPreview.tsx:69 `case "summary": return true` (every other case tests a non-empty result array, e.g. :75 `case "features": return result.feature_importance.length > 0`); :81 `const activeTab = availableTabs.includes(tab) ? tab : "summary"`.
- Second pass: The code facts hold — frontend/src/panels/ModellingPreview.tsx:69 `case "summary": return true` while every other case tests a non-empty array (e.g. :75, :77), and :81 `const activeTab = availableTabs.includes(tab) ? tab : "summary"` makes Summary the fallback; the reset clause at high-level.md:29 is confirmed by :59-60 `useEffect(() => setTab("summary"), [result])`. But the finding overstates: Summary is still result-backed in the sense that ModellingPreview only renders when a result exists, so "only result-backed tabs" is an acceptable component-level abstraction; what is genuinely undocumented is that Summary is unconditional and is the fallback, and the low-level document does not state it either (no mention at low-level.md around the ModellingPreview control flow). P3 ambiguity at most, not an inaccuracy.
- Fix: State that Summary is always present for any result and acts as the fallback tab, while the remaining tabs each require their own non-empty result array.

- Problem: The bare token `cross_validation` is also the live value of `evaluation.validation.method`, which does have a dedicated editor in the Split pane. - Second pass: Both cited facts check out: frontend/src/panels/modelling/SplitAndMetricsConfig.tsx:37 `{ value: "cross_validation", label: "Cross-validation" }` and :274 `{validationMethod === "cross_validation" && (` render a fold-count editor, and low-level.md:253 plus high-level.md:170-171 describe the Cross-validation control. - Fix: Disambiguate by naming the location: refer to the retired *top-level node config keys* `config.split`/`config.cross_validation`, explicitly distinguishing them from the `evaluation.validation.method` value `cross_validation`, which is editable in the Split pane.

### F192 frontend-preview-explore `specs/frontend-preview-explore/low-level.md:222` stale
- Spec: The later pipeline-recovery and engineering-quality contracts own the increases from this chart package's 258/1,300 KiB baseline; chart capability still pays its cost only after Charts is opened and cannot quietly grow inside the aggregate budget.
- Problem: This attribution of the growth above the chart package's baseline is incomplete and now misleading: two later, unrelated increases (submodel boundary staleness fingerprints and the collapsed submodel input socket) raised the initial ceiling from 279 to 283 KiB and are owned by neither named contract. As written a reader attributing current headroom would blame the wrong packages.
- Evidence: frontend/scripts/check-bundle-size.mjs:79-90 attributes the 279→281 raise to "Structural staleness fingerprints and deferred shared-node deletion cleanup for the boundary-editing hook" and 281→283 to "The collapsed submodel input socket", neither of which is the pipeline-recovery or engineering-quality contract named in the spec.
- Second pass: specs/frontend-preview-explore/low-level.md:221-223 attributes all growth above the chart package's 258/1,300 baseline to the pipeline-recovery and engineering-quality contracts, but frontend/scripts/check-bundle-size.mjs:78-90 attributes the last two raises (279->281, 281->283) to the boundary-editing staleness fingerprints and the collapsed submodel input socket, neither of which is a named contract. The claim is genuinely incomplete, though the practical harm is limited to mis-attributed headroom rather than a wrong number, so P3 (the wrong-number part is already carried by F083).
- Fix: Drop the historical ownership clause and state only the present fact: the chart runtime/vendor is excluded from the initial budget and capped separately at 205 KiB gzip, so chart growth cannot hide inside the aggregate ceiling.

### F196 git-integration `specs/git-integration/low-level.md:144` missing
- Spec: → `git add -- <paths>` → `git commit -m <msg> -- <paths>` (pathspec-scoped, so it commits only those paths' working-tree state regardless of what else the user may have pre-staged) → returns the new SHA.
- Problem: The Save control flow omits a second idempotency gate that runs between `add` and `commit` and can return `None` after staging has already occurred. A reader would conclude that a non-empty `status --porcelain` always produces a commit.
- Evidence: src/haute/_git_transactions.py:80-82 runs `git diff --cached --quiet HEAD -- <paths>` after the add and returns `None` when clean, because a stale index entry can make porcelain report `MM` for content identical to HEAD.
- Second pass: src/haute/_git_transactions.py:80-82 runs `git diff --cached --quiet HEAD -- <paths>` after the add and returns None when clean, with a comment about a stale index entry reporting a spurious MM. The Save chain at specs/git-integration/low-level.md:141-147 goes straight from `git add` to `git commit`, so the second idempotency gate is genuinely undocumented. Omission of one internal step, not a contract error — P3 is appropriate.
- Fix: Add the post-add `git diff --cached --quiet HEAD -- <paths>` re-check to the chain and note that it, too, returns `None` (a stale index entry can report a spurious modification).

### F197 git-integration `specs/git-integration/low-level.md:410` structure (partial)
- Spec: **Historical extraction (`archive_commit` / `commit_pipeline_graph`).**
- Problem: `commit_pipeline_graph` is named as if it belonged to this component, but it lives in a file that appears nowhere in this Module map, so the reference is an ambiguous bare symbol under R5 and misattributes ownership of the parse step.

- Second pass: The factual half holds: commit_pipeline_graph resolves only at src/haute/routes/_helpers.py:978 (imported by src/haute/routes/git.py:56) and src/haute/routes/_helpers.py appears nowhere in this component's Module map (grep for `_helpers.py` in the spec hits only tests/_git_helpers.py at line 524). The rule citation is a stretch: the governance rule constrains `path::symbol` references that must resolve, and line 410 uses a bare symbol name, which is not itself banned; the real issue is mild attribution ambiguity, so P3 stands but not as an R5 breach.
- Fix: Write it as `src/haute/routes/_helpers.py::commit_pipeline_graph` and state that the parse step is owned by the server-api/helpers layer, while `src/haute/_git_history.py::archive_commit` owns the bounded extraction.

### F147 governance `specs/README.md:67` accuracy (partial)
- Spec: An undeclared `.md` or `.toml` anywhere below `specs/` fails inventory rather than disappearing.
- Problem: The gate does not apply to files directly under specs/: any .md or .toml at the specs/ root is accepted unconditionally as a "governance" document with no declaration in corpus.toml. Only nested component documents (and non-Markdown-suffixed files) actually fail. The claim overstates the enforcement a reviewer would rely on.
- Evidence: scripts/spec_corpus_inventory.py:170-172 appends every file directly under specs/ whose suffix is in SUPPORTED_SPEC_SUFFIXES as kind "governance" with no manifest lookup; the undeclared check at :202-204 then only reports paths not already discovered, so a new specs/anything.md is silently classified rather than failing.
- Second pass: specs/README.md:66-68 does say "An undeclared `.md` or `.toml` anywhere below `specs/` fails inventory rather than disappearing", and scripts/spec_corpus_inventory.py:175-179 does append every file directly under specs/ with a supported suffix as kind "governance" with no corpus.toml lookup, so a new specs/anything.md is auto-classified rather than failing. But the finding overstates: the sentence's stated consequence ("rather than disappearing") still holds — the root file is discovered and reported in the root specification-governance tier that the immediately preceding sentence (specs/README.md:63-64) names, and component high/low pairs and roadmap .md files are likewise auto-discovered without declaration. The real defect is only the loose word "undeclared"; P3 is right.
- Fix: Say that undeclared component-level and nested documents fail inventory, and that root-level specification-governance documents are an accepted tier (or tighten the script to a closed root allow-list).

### F202 io-layer `specs/io-layer/low-level.md:118` consistency
- Spec: `ready`/`fresh` and `ready`/`unknown` reuse; `missing` builds; `ready`/`stale` refreshes; `corrupt` raises `SourceCacheCorruptError`.

A reader of the low-level document would conclude a stale-signature input with a deleted source is always refreshed.
- Evidence: src/haute/_input_preparation.py:379-396 logs `input_snapshot_source_unavailable`, records `warning_code="source_unavailable"` when a published generation exists, and otherwise raises with `reason_code="build_failed"`. - Second pass: specs/io-layer/low-level.md:116-118 enumerates status handling exactly as quoted and contains no `source_unavailable` branch (grep over the file finds the token nowhere), while src/haute/_input_preparation.py:379-400 reuses a published generation with `warning_code="source_unavailable"` when the source signature is `missing` and otherwise raises `reason_code="build_failed"`, and specs/io-layer/high-level.md:68-70 documents that behaviour. The omission is real but the enumeration is not stated as exhaustive and the sibling high-level document is correct, so P3 rather than P2.
- Fix: Add the missing-source branch to step 2 of "Automatic preparation": a `missing` source signature with a published generation reuses it with `warning_code="source_unavailable"` and never refreshes; without a generation preparation is refused as `build_failed`.

### F207 mlflow-model-registry `specs/mlflow-model-registry/low-level.md:325` consistency
- Spec: **Disk-cache filenames are sha256 digests of the artifact path**, not the artifact's own basename
- Problem: The digest is the *directory* name, not the file name; the cached file is always named `artifact` plus the artifact's extension. The same document's Control flow describes this correctly, so the two statements disagree.
- Evidence: src/haute/_mlflow_io.py:272-277 in `_artifact_cache_path`: `digest = sha256(artifact_path.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()`, `file_name = f"artifact{suffix}" if suffix else "artifact"`, `candidate = (cache_root / run_id / digest / file_name).resolve()`. Contrast specs/mlflow-model-registry/low-level.md:143-145 ("sha256-digest of `artifact_path` as the directory name, extension-preserving file name").
- Second pass: src/haute/_mlflow_io.py:269-279 `_artifact_cache_path`: `digest = sha256(artifact_path...).hexdigest()`, `file_name = f"artifact{suffix}"`, `candidate = (cache_root / run_id / digest / file_name)` — the digest is the directory component and the file is always `artifact[.ext]`. The invariant heading at line 325 ("Disk-cache filenames are sha256 digests") is literally wrong and disagrees with Control flow at lines 143-146; the collision-avoidance substance it argues still holds, so P3.
- Fix: Reword the invariant to "Disk-cache directory names are sha256 digests of the artifact path" so it matches Control flow and the code.

### F210 modelling `specs/modelling/high-level.md:142` accuracy
- Spec: training rejects a non-object mapping, malformed names or directions, active constraints on absent/non-selected features, and constraints on categorical, Boolean, temporal, or otherwise non-numeric features before splitting or fitting.
- Problem: The implementation is stricter than 'non-numeric': it accepts only the dtype names `Int64` and `Float64`, so a numeric feature carried as e.g. Float32/Int32 is also rejected. A reader would expect any numeric dtype to be allowed.
- Evidence: src/haute/modelling/_training_job.py:2444-2452 rejects every constrained feature whose `prepared.feature_dtypes` entry is not in `{"Int64", "Float64"}`, with the message 'monotone_constraints require numeric Int64 or Float64 features'.
- Second pass: specs/modelling/high-level.md:141-144 says constraints are rejected on "categorical, Boolean, temporal, or otherwise non-numeric features", but src/haute/modelling/_training_job.py:2442-2452 rejects every constrained feature whose prepared.feature_dtypes entry is not in {"Int64", "Float64"}, so a Float32/Int32 feature is rejected despite being numeric. Genuine but low-impact ambiguity; P3 is right.
- Fix: Say constraints are accepted only on `Int64`/`Float64` features and rejected on every other dtype.

### F110 modelling `specs/modelling/low-level.md:211` structure (partial)
- Spec: it takes the budget envelope with `_execution_admission.py::isolated_execution_budget`
- Problem: R5 requires exact repository-root-relative paths and forbids ambiguous basenames. Three modules are cited by bare filename, and the surrounding prose (all other route modules are given as `src/haute/routes/...`) implies they live under `src/haute/routes/`, where they do not exist.
- Evidence: `src/haute/routes/_execution_admission.py` does not exist; the symbol is at src/haute/_execution_admission.py:255. Same for `_worker_isolation.py::isolated_worker_memory_detail` (spec line 277) at src/haute/_worker_isolation.py:292 and `_worker_protocol.WORKER_USER_MESSAGE_FIELD` (spec line 707) at src/haute/_worker_protocol.py:54.
- Second pass: The three symbols do resolve, but only at src/haute/_execution_admission.py:255, src/haute/_worker_isolation.py:292 and src/haute/_worker_protocol.py:54, and the spec cites them as bare basenames (`_execution_admission.py::isolated_execution_budget` at low-level.md:212-213, not 211 as cited), which R5 discourages when a path is available. The finding overstates in two ways: the cited line is off by one to two lines, and the surrounding prose does not imply `src/haute/routes/` — the same section cites the genuine routes module as bare `_training_lifecycle.py::TrainService._execute_and_sink` (low-level.md:210), so bare basenames are the section's uniform style rather than a routes-directory implication. Formatting nit, not a factual error.
- Fix: Write the three references as `src/haute/_execution_admission.py::isolated_execution_budget`, `src/haute/_worker_isolation.py::isolated_worker_memory_detail`, and `src/haute/_worker_protocol.py::WORKER_USER_MESSAGE_FIELD`.

### F213 modelling `specs/modelling/low-level.md:331` governance (partial)
- Spec: Direct/test callers that deliberately omit it retain the constructor-only legacy split/CV pipeline described above.
- Problem: Labels a live code path 'legacy'. R1 forbids legacy-path language; the path is current behaviour reached whenever `evaluation` is omitted, and the Module map already frames `_split.py` as a private test seam rather than legacy.
- Evidence: src/haute/modelling/_training_job.py:548 `run()` branches to `self._split_data(...)` at :619 when no evaluation plan is present; src/haute/modelling/_split.py is present and exercised.
- Second pass: The word "legacy" at specs/modelling/low-level.md:331 does label a live path — src/haute/modelling/_training_job.py:548 run() still calls self._split_data(...) at :619 when no evaluation plan is supplied, and src/haute/modelling/_split.py exists. But the sentence does not narrate a superseded state or a past change; it describes the current fallback and explicitly scopes it to direct/test callers, so it is a wording/consistency nit rather than a clear R1 legacy-path violation. P3, not a governance defect.
- Fix: Call it the internal/test-seam split pipeline, consistent with the Module map line for `src/haute/modelling/_split.py`.

### F211 modelling `specs/modelling/high-level.md:464` structure (partial)
- Spec: ## Unified evaluation and bounded tuning
- Problem: high-level.md carries an extra top-level section beyond the template's six headings, and its `### Regression evidence` subsection (line 636) enumerates test coverage, which belongs in low-level.md's `## Testing`. It also embeds two JSON config blocks, in tension with the no-long-excerpt rule.
- Evidence: specs/TEMPLATE.md:12-37 defines the high-level heading set (Purpose, Scope, Behaviour, Design rationale, Interactions, Failure model) and TEMPLATE.md:83 prohibits long code excerpts; TEMPLATE.md:65 assigns test strategy/scenarios to low-level.md.
- Second pass: The extra top-level heading is real: specs/modelling/high-level.md carries `## Purpose`(3), `## Scope`(22), `## Behaviour`(67), `## Design rationale`(219), `## Interactions`(336), `## Failure model`(350) and then `## Unified evaluation and bounded tuning`(464), beyond the six headings TEMPLATE.md:12-37 defines. The two sub-claims are weak: TEMPLATE.md:83 prohibits long *code* excerpts and the blocks are short canonical config shapes, and no rule forbids a high-level summary of regression evidence — TEMPLATE.md only assigns the detailed test strategy to low-level.md. Structural nit only, P3.
- Fix: Fold the behavioural contract into Behaviour/Failure model, move the regression-evidence paragraphs into low-level.md's Testing section, and shorten the JSON blocks to prose descriptions of the required keys.

### F216 pipeline-config `specs/pipeline-config/low-level.md:37` missing
- Spec: **`NodeRegistry`** — base class for `Pipeline` and `Submodel`; holds `_nodes: list[Node]`, `_node_map: dict[str, Node]`, `_edges: list[RegisteredEdge]`, `_submodel_files: list[str]`.
- Problem: The field enumeration is incomplete: `_submodel_registrations` is omitted, and the `RegisteredSubmodel` dataclass it holds is absent from Key types entirely even though its sibling `RegisteredEdge` is documented. Since `_register_node` also validates against these registrations, a reader modelling the registry state would miss a live invariant.
- Evidence: src/haute/pipeline.py:211-219 — `__init__` sets `self.name`, `self.description`, `_nodes`, `_node_map`, `_edges`, `_submodel_files`, and `self._submodel_registrations: list[RegisteredSubmodel] = []`. `RegisteredSubmodel` is a frozen dataclass declared at src/haute/pipeline.py:186. src/haute/pipeline.py:228-244 rejects a node whose `__name__` matches a registered submodel `alias` or `instance_id` with `ValueError`.
- Second pass: specs/pipeline-config/low-level.md:37-38 lists only `_nodes`, `_node_map`, `_edges`, `_submodel_files`, but src/haute/pipeline.py:211-218 also sets `_submodel_registrations: list[RegisteredSubmodel]`; `RegisteredSubmodel` (frozen dataclass, src/haute/pipeline.py:185-194) is absent from Key types while its sibling `RegisteredEdge` is documented, and src/haute/pipeline.py:228-241 enforces a live invariant against those registrations.
- Fix: Add `_submodel_registrations: list[RegisteredSubmodel]` to the field list, document `RegisteredSubmodel` as a Key type, and add the submodel-identity `ValueError` to the Error handling `ValueError` bullet.

### F220 rating `specs/rating/low-level.md:79` accuracy
- Spec: c. Build a `pl.DataFrame(entries)`; return unchanged if it has no `"value"` column; reject (raise `ValueError`) any NaN/Infinity or null `value` entries. ... e. Resolve and validate each originating input dtype.
- Problem: The documented ordering does not match the code: the entry-column presence check (missing `value` key or missing factor key) runs before `defaultValue` parsing, and originating-dtype validation runs before the entries `DataFrame` is built and before the NaN/Inf/null value rejection. Which error a malformed table surfaces therefore differs from the spec's order (an unsupported factor dtype wins over a NaN entry value).
- Evidence: src/haute/_rating.py:938-945 entry-column passthrough check; :947-965 default parsing; :966-970 `_validate_supported_factor_dtypes`; :972-996 `pl.DataFrame(entries)` build and NaN/Inf then null rejection.
- Second pass: specs/rating/low-level.md:79-84 orders: (c) build `pl.DataFrame(entries)` + value-column check + NaN/Inf/null rejection, (d) project `[*factors, "value"]` with the factor-absent guard, (e) resolve/validate originating dtypes. src/haute/_rating.py:938-945 runs the entry-column/factor-key passthrough guard first, :947-965 parses `defaultValue`, :966-970 calls `_validate_supported_factor_dtypes`, and only :972-996 builds the entries DataFrame and rejects NaN/Inf then null values. So an unsupported factor dtype raises before a NaN entry value, contrary to the documented order.
- Fix: Reorder steps b-e to: entry-column passthrough guard → default parsing → originating-dtype resolution/validation → entries DataFrame build with NaN/Inf and null `value` rejection → `[*factors, "value"]` projection.

### F219 rating `specs/rating/high-level.md:113` accuracy (partial)
- Spec: After the table stage, `combinedOutputs` combine named table output columns with a required fixed finite numeric `baseValue`
- Problem: A combined output does not name its participants: every combined output combines the same shared list of all materialised table output columns plus the base literal. "named table output columns" suggests a per-combine participant list that the config does not have.
- Evidence: src/haute/_rating.py:1368-1376 `_apply_rating_step_outputs` passes the single accumulated `out_cols` list to `_combine_rating_output` for every entry in `combined_outputs`; `_normalise_combined_outputs` (src/haute/_rating.py:1204-1253) accepts only `outputColumn`, `operation`, `baseValue`.
- Second pass: Code side holds: src/haute/_rating.py:1369-1376 passes the single accumulated `out_cols` list to `_combine_rating_output` for every combined entry, and `_normalise_combined_outputs` (1204-1253) accepts only outputColumn/operation/baseValue, so there is no per-combine participant list. But the finding overstates the misleading effect: the very next sentences of specs/rating/high-level.md:111-116 say a table that never materialised its output column "is omitted from combining", which already implies every materialised table column participates, so "named table output columns" reads as the table-named columns rather than a per-combine selection. Real but minor wording imprecision, P3.
- Fix: Reword to "combine every materialised table output column of the step with a required fixed finite numeric `baseValue`".

### F224 reference-pipeline `specs/reference-pipeline/low-level.md:63` accuracy
- Spec: The shared helper validates the sidecar, resolves its data path within the project boundary, and calls `load_v2_api_source()` to yield the multi-table lazy input.
- Problem: The ordering is reversed relative to the code: path anchoring/project-boundary enforcement runs first, and only then is the v2 `tables` shape checked and the schema validated. This determines which error an engineer sees first — a path escaping the project root raises before any schema error is reported. The same reversed order appears in high-level.md:55-57.
- Evidence: src/haute/_node_apply.py:91-92 resolves the config then calls `_anchored_required_path(config, base_dir)` (which does `resolve_runtime_file_path(..., enforce_project_root=True)` at src/haute/_node_apply.py:70-76); only afterwards, at src/haute/_node_apply.py:101 and :108, does it check `isinstance(config.get("tables"), list)` and call `validate_v2_schema(config)`.
- Second pass: src/haute/_node_apply.py:91-92 calls _resolve_node_config then _anchored_required_path (which does resolve_runtime_file_path(..., enforce_project_root=True) at :63-77) before the tables check at :100-107 and validate_v2_schema at :108, so the boundary-anchored path resolution genuinely precedes sidecar/v2 validation. The spec's ordering at low-level.md:63 (and the same ordering in high-level.md:55-57) is reversed; impact is limited to which error a reader expects first, so P3 is right.
- Fix: Reorder both descriptions: the helper anchors and project-boundary-checks the sidecar's `path` first, then rejects an absent/non-list `tables`, then validates the v2 schema, then calls `load_v2_api_source()`.

### F225 reference-pipeline `specs/reference-pipeline/low-level.md:105` accuracy
- Spec: the current missing quote data is detected by `load_v2_api_source()` when it stats the resolved data path.
- Problem: No stat of the data path happens on the cold (uncached) path. `load_v2_api_source()` only computes a file signature when plausible cache metadata already exists; with no cache it delegates to the direct-spill shredder, and the FileNotFoundError comes from opening the file for record iteration.
- Evidence: src/haute/_json_shred/_cache.py:769-776 calls `_source_proof._data_file_signature(Path(data_path))` only inside `if plausible_meta:`; with no cache dir the loop `continue`s and src/haute/_json_shred/_cache.py:840 calls `_writer._shred_data_file_to_direct_spill(Path(data_path), ...)`, which iterates `_records._iter_records(data_path)` (src/haute/_json_shred/_writer.py:309); src/haute/_json_shred/_records.py:436 does `data_path.open("rb")` — an open, not a stat.
- Second pass: src/haute/_json_shred/_cache.py:748-772 comments that a cold execution deliberately does not hash/stat the source first and computes _data_file_signature only inside `if plausible_meta:`; with no cache the loop continues to :840 _shred_data_file_to_direct_spill, whose record iteration opens the file (src/haute/_json_shred/_records.py:436 data_path.open("rb")). So low-level.md:105's "when it stats the resolved data path" (and the matching "filesystem metadata read" at line ~66) misdescribes the mechanism, though the error still surfaces from inside load_v2_api_source().
- Fix: Say the missing data file surfaces as FileNotFoundError when the shared loader opens the resolved source for record iteration (no cache layer can serve the schema), rather than describing a filesystem metadata read.

- Second pass: The alias is real and ordinary (src/haute/_trace_correlation.py:35), and `src/haute/_json_safe.py` is named in both specs/json-shredding/low-level.md and the tracing Module map cell at low-level.md:8 with no record in specs/ownership.toml — that half stands under the toml preamble ("mandatory when multiple Module maps name the file"). The R1 half is a stretch: "compatibility name" is loose wording, not a narration of a superseded state or a backward-compatibility path, so it is at most a P3 clarity issue rather than a governance violation.
- Fix: Say "imported locally as `_jsonify_row`" (drop "compatibility name"), and either drop the cross-component path or record the consumer relationship in specs/ownership.toml.

### F234 tracing `specs/tracing/low-level.md:163` accuracy
- Spec: `_enrich_steps()` (from `_trace_enrichment.py`, passed `source_frames_of`) enriches every step in place.
- Problem: `_trace_enrichment.py` exports `enrich_steps`; `_enrich_steps` is only the local alias created by the import in `trace.py`. As a `path::symbol`-style reference it does not resolve in the named file, and the same document names the function correctly as `enrich_steps` elsewhere (lines 10, 295). The call also passes `incoming_edges_of`, which the control-flow step omits.
- Evidence: src/haute/_trace_enrichment.py:1420 `def enrich_steps(`; src/haute/trace.py:81 `from haute._trace_enrichment import enrich_steps as _enrich_steps`; trace.py:706-715 passes `source_frames_of=` and `incoming_edges_of=`.
- Second pass: src/haute/_trace_enrichment.py:1420 defines `enrich_steps`; `_enrich_steps` exists only as the import alias at src/haute/trace.py:81, so low-level.md:163's "`_enrich_steps()` (from `_trace_enrichment.py`...)" does not resolve in the named file, and trace.py:706-715 also passes `incoming_edges_of=` which the step omits. Minor, correctly rated P3.
- Fix: Write "`enrich_steps()` (imported into `trace.py` as `_enrich_steps`)" and mention the `incoming_edges_of` argument.

- [P2] cli `specs/cli/low-level.md:78`: The placeholder directory names are wrong: the scaffold creates `models/` and `outputs/`, not `model/` and `output/`. - [P2] tracing `specs/tracing/high-level.md:180`: No `invalidate()` method exists on the trace cache; the retraining path calls `clear()`. This is a separate error from the wrong module path in F141 and would survive that finding's suggested fix. - [P2] tracing `specs/tracing/high-level.md:294`: Second, independent occurrence of the same wrong module and wrong method in the Interactions section (the Behaviour section occurrence is F141), so a fix limited to line 180 leaves the Interactions contract wrong. Evidence: src/haute/routes/_training_lifecycle.py contains no reference to trace (grep returns nothing); the importer is src/haute/routes/_training_preparation.py:686, which calls `.clear()` at :699.
- [P2] frontend-shared `specs/frontend-shared/low-level.md:20`: The Module map entry enumerates the store's caches as preview/solve/train/explore and omits the pivot result and pivot job caches entirely, even though it goes on to describe the pivot start-claim machinery — a third independent location (after high-level.md:167 and low-level.md:135) where the pivot cache is dropped from an enumeration that reads as exhaustive. Evidence: frontend/src/stores/useNodeResultsStore.ts:752-753 declares `pivotResults: Record<string, CachedExplorePivotResult>` and `pivotJobs: Record<string, ActiveExplorePivotJob>` alongside the four listed caches, and :42/:701-726 give the pivot cache its own bound and LRU trim.
- [P2] frontend-shared `specs/frontend-shared/low-level.md:302`: This invariant is stated for the store's eviction behaviour generally, but the pivot cache's trimmer does not honour it: pinned pivot keys are merely ordered last and are evicted once the unpinned keys run out, so the pinned Explore node's pivot matrices can be dropped under pressure. The spec gives no hint that a second, weaker eviction rule exists. Evidence: frontend/src/stores/useNodeResultsStore.ts:718-726: `evictionOrder` concatenates unpinned keys then `keys.filter((key) => protectedKeys.has(key)).sort(byRecency)` and then `.slice(0, evictCount)`, so protected keys are deleted when `evictCount` exceeds the unpinned count — unlike `trimCacheByRecency` (:689) which filters `key !== pinnedKey` out of the candidate list entirely.
- [P2] frontend-graph-canvas `specs/frontend-graph-canvas/low-level.md:1492`: low-level.md carries a seventh top-level heading beyond the six specs/TEMPLATE.md:41-68 fixes (Module map, Key types and data structures, Control flow, Edge cases and invariants, Error handling, Testing). The first pass flagged only the equivalent extra heading in high-level.md (F067) and missed the identical violation in the sibling document; its content (adapter behaviour, banner/panel wiring, snapshot retention) belongs under Control flow and Edge cases and invariants. Evidence: frontend/src/types/node.ts:6-26 defines exactly 19 PIPELINE_NODE_TYPES entries with `dataInput`/`dataOutput` and no Data Source/Data Sink identifiers; specs/TEMPLATE.md:72-76 requires resolved history folded into present-tense prose.
- [P2] io-layer `specs/io-layer/low-level.md:321`: Resolved-history language: "still require" plus "those rules no longer bound" narrates a superseded constraint as a past change, which the writing rules forbid (fold resolved history into present-tense prose). Evidence: specs/io-layer/low-level.md:320-322; the same paragraph's present-tense facts (snapshot builds use `infer_schema_length=None`, prefer the scanner, record `eager_read_mode_scanned`) are verifiable in src/haute/_input_providers.py / _polars_io_registry.py without the "no longer" framing, so the history clause carries no current information.

## Refuted by the second pass

- F001 frontend-shared `specs/frontend-shared/low-level.md:660` (P1 accuracy): The finding's central code claim is wrong against the on-disk tree: frontend/src/hooks/usePipelineAPI.ts:1195 is `base_revision: saveSourceRevision || null` (logical OR, not `?? null`), so an empty-string ref is coerced to `null` and a never-persisted document does send `null`. The spec quote is also a misquote: specs/frontend-shared/low-level.md:660-661 actually reads "sends `sourceRevisionRef.current` as `base_revision`; the ref holds an empty string for a document that has never been persisted, which is sent as `null`" — an accurate description of the shipped code. The Testing bullet at low-level.md:453 is therefore also correct.
- F015 assistant `specs/assistant/low-level.md:222` (P2 governance): The spec matches the code's own terminology, which TEMPLATE.md:105-106 requires: src/haute/assistant/_tools.py:709 docstring reads "Return the manifest-backed legacy node-catalogue compatibility view" (the finding cited only _catalog.py:1042 and omitted this), and low-level.md:1002's "legacy exemplar" mirrors `legacy_examples` at src/haute/assistant/_assets.py:135/150. Neither sentence narrates a superseded state or a past change, so the strict resolved-history rule is not engaged.
- F076 frontend-modelling-optimiser-ui `specs/frontend-modelling-optimiser-ui/low-level.md:49` (P2 accuracy): specs/frontend-modelling-optimiser-ui/low-level.md:49 reads exactly as quoted, and it is literally true for this component: grep over frontend/src finds no top-level `split`/`cross_validation` config key written or read (only `validation_method` unions in frontend/src/api/types.ts:539,728), so there is neither an editor nor a runtime result path. src/haute/modelling/_train_config.py:332-337 does raise TrainingConfigError, but the spec nowhere claims the keys are accepted or silently ignored, and a backend train-config parse contract belongs to the modelling component's spec, not a frontend UI document's Error handling section.
- F123 reference-pipeline `specs/reference-pipeline/high-level.md:104` (P2 missing): specs/reference-pipeline/high-level.md:102-105 explicitly scopes out rating/config/expander/premium.json as an untracked artifact (git ls-files rating shows no config/expander/), and the load_error behaviour the finding cites (src/haute/parser.py:72-81, :188, :198; src/haute/_config_io.py:336) is parser/pipeline-config behaviour, not behaviour this reference component owns. Declining to promise behaviour for a file the component does not ship is an acceptable component-level abstraction, not a Failure-model omission.
- F125 roadmap `specs/roadmap/engineering-quality.md:138` (P2 missing): The quote at engineering-quality.md:138 is accurate and `grep -n workflow_coverage specs/roadmap/*.md` is indeed empty, but the finding's decisive claim — that ENG-T01 was 'retired without leaving the artefact identified anywhere in the corpus' — is false: specs/engineering-quality/high-level.md:59 and :65 name `tests/workflow_coverage.toml` and its validator `tests/test_workflow_coverage.py`, and specs/engineering-quality/low-level.md:100-101, :187 and :482 record both in the Module map and Testing sections. 'The coverage ledger' therefore resolves for a reader through the owning component spec; naming a path in the roadmap too is a nicety, not a governance breach.
- F133 server-api `specs/server-api/low-level.md:342` (P2 missing): specs/server-api/low-level.md:345-351 already states the precondition "digests every owned artifact it can observe (the parent source and sidecar, everything under `modules/` and `config/` beneath the pipeline root); every staged write or delete in steps 5, 7, 9, and 10 first re-checks that the artifact's current bytes, or absence, still equal that observation and raises the same conflict before the artifact changes." That is precisely the behaviour the finding claims is undocumented.
- F160 caching `specs/caching/low-level.md:217` (P3 test-ref): The premise that the three modules are "named in no Testing bullet" is false at repository scope: `tests/test_json_cache_cross_process.py` is claimed at specs/json-shredding/low-level.md:792, `tests/test_json_cache_coverage_uplift.py` at specs/json-shredding/low-level.md:800, and `tests/test_execute_lazy_dataframe_cache.py` at specs/execution-engine/low-level.md:1492. The governance rule requires that files named in Testing resolve, not that every related test be listed; ownership sits with the neighbouring components, exactly the alternative the finding's own suggested fix allows.
- F161 cli `specs/cli/low-level.md:64` (P3 accuracy): The quoted row (specs/cli/low-level.md:64) asserts only an exit code, not a formatted message, and an uncaught `ConfigError` from src/haute/_project.py:125,133 still terminates with exit 1. The spec already documents this class of propagation explicitly at specs/cli/low-level.md:234-235 ("Several configuration, SDK, I/O, scoring, and programming errors are outside these formatting catches and propagate with their original exception/traceback (still exit 1)"), so no spec statement is contradicted.
- F168 deploy `specs/deploy/low-level.md:206` (P3 accuracy): specs/deploy/low-level.md:206-208 says dispatch routes "any other `_CONTAINER_BASED_TARGETS` member (`azure-container-apps`, `aws-ecs`, `gcp-run`)" — semantically exactly the branch at src/haute/deploy/__init__.py:80, since :49 defines `_CONTAINER_PLATFORM_TARGETS = _CONTAINER_BASED_TARGETS - {"container"}` and `"container"` is handled by the preceding branch. The spec never claims the constant is defined in `__init__.py`, and `_CONTAINER_BASED_TARGETS` resolves (src/haute/deploy/_config.py:35). Naming the derived set would be marginally friendlier, but nothing is inaccurate.
- F172 engineering-quality `specs/engineering-quality/low-level.md:27` (P3 structure): The Module map (specs/engineering-quality/low-level.md:5-6) has no preamble claiming an exhaustive frontend/e2e inventory, and the omission follows the TEMPLATE.md primary-owner rule: `frontend/e2e/explore.spec.ts` is named in exactly one Module map (specs/frontend-preview-explore/low-level.md:24), so the coverage rule is satisfied and no specs/ownership.toml entry is due. The counter-examples cited are not analogous — `edge-join.spec.ts` and `canvas-assurance.spec.ts` appear in only this Module map (other components mention them in prose, e.g. specs/frontend-graph-canvas/low-level.md:1478), so engineering-quality is their primary owner. No spec statement is inaccurate.
- F191 frontend-node-editors `specs/frontend-node-editors/high-level.md:259` (P3 governance): The central evidence is misread: `baseInput`/`joinInput` are live referents in the current tree — frontend/src/utils/edgeJoinValidation.ts:93-94 explicitly detects those config keys and emits "Edge Join input roles are stored on incoming edge handles; remove legacy baseInput/joinInput config.", covered by frontend/src/utils/__tests__/edgeJoinValidation.test.ts:163-169 and frontend/src/__tests__/editors/EdgeJoinEditor.test.tsx:411-418. high-level.md:259 therefore states a present, code-backed rule (handles are the sole authority; no compatibility path) rather than narrating a superseded state; the residual "now" at low-level.md:569 and the v1-migration denial at line 667 are mild wording at most, not the P3 legacy-path violation claimed.
- F205 mlflow-model-registry `specs/mlflow-model-registry/low-level.md:7` (P3 accuracy): Deploy does use `_score_eager`: src/haute/deploy/_scorer.py:908,925 calls `haute._model_scorer._run_score_pipeline`, and that function (defined at src/haute/_model_scorer.py:1039) imports and invokes `_score_eager` at :1081. The Module-map phrasing "used by both `_model_scorer.py` and deploy" is an accurate component-level statement of who exercises the delegate, not a claim of a direct import in `src/haute/deploy/`.
- F212 modelling `specs/modelling/low-level.md:25` (P3 governance): specs/modelling/low-level.md:25 accurately describes the code as it is: src/haute/routes/_train_service.py's own docstring is "Compatibility facade for the decomposed training service." and the file is pure re-exports. R1 bars a spec from describing a *backward-compatibility path* as current behaviour; here the spec is faithfully documenting a live internal import seam that the code itself names that way, and the line already states what it does and does not own. The complaint is about the code's design, not a spec-versus-code inaccuracy.
- F222 reference-pipeline `specs/reference-pipeline/low-level.md:11` (P3 accuracy): The quoted module-map line describes only rating/config/quote_response/Quote_Response_9.json, and that file genuinely ends with "contract": {"inputs": [], "outputs": []} (lines 53-56); the spec nowhere claims the @pipeline.output decorator contract is empty, so nothing in the spec is contradicted by rating/main.py:19. This is an omission-of-context complaint about a factually correct sentence.
