- mlflow-model-registry `specs/mlflow-model-registry/low-level.md:8`: `src/haute/_mlflow_utils.py` is "used by `_mlflow_io.py`, the optimiser IO layer, and deploy's bundler" (Verified the module's symbols exist (`search_versions`, `resolve_version`, `allow_file_store_if_local`, `resolve_mlflow_source`) but did not enumerate every importer across the optimiser and deploy packages within budget.)
- mlflow-model-registry `specs/mlflow-model-registry/low-level.md:8`: `resolve_mlflow_source` and `allow_file_store_if_local` coverage: the Module map omits `allow_file_store_if_local`, which `routes/mlflow.py` calls during tracking setup (The Module map line is a one-line responsibility summary, so the omission may be intentional; not reported as a finding.)
- mlflow-model-registry `specs/mlflow-model-registry/high-level.md:194`: `_local_artifact_fingerprint` "reuses the deploy path's `artifact_identity_fingerprint`" (Verified accurate (src/haute/_mlflow_io.py:358 imports it from `haute.deploy._scorer`), but note the function lives in `src/haute/deploy/_scorer.py`, a file this component's Scope section does not list among the deploy files it names; no rule requires it, so not reported.)
- optimiser `specs/optimiser/low-level.md:266`: spawns a daemon thread running `_run_frontier_sweep` inside `solver_worker_context()` (Slightly imprecise rather than wrong: the thread target is `_frontier_background`, and `solver_worker_context()` is entered inside `_run_frontier_sweep` around the `_compute_frontier` call only (src/haute/routes/optimiser.py:1610-1622), not around the parent-job update or the lifecycle transitions. The Key-types section already describes the correct mechanism, so I did not raise it as a contradiction.)
- optimiser `specs/optimiser/high-level.md:324`: Online trace correlation always reads the quote id from the artifact-configured quote-id column, including when the output row also contains a different literal `quote_id` field. (Plausible and consistent with the module's explicit-empty-config rejection, but I did not read the specific correlation branch in `_explain_online` closely enough to confirm the precedence rule against a literal `quote_id` output field.)
- frontend-graph-canvas `specs/frontend-graph-canvas/low-level.md:1492`: The document carries a seventh top-level section, `## Recovery implementation contract`, after `## Testing`; high-level.md likewise carries `## Recovery canvas behaviour` after `## Failure model`. (R4 enumerates the required headings but neither specs/TEMPLATE.md nor the rules given prohibit an additional section, and the same pattern appears in the paired high-level document, suggesting it is an accepted corpus convention rather than drift. Not reported as a finding.)
- frontend-graph-canvas `specs/frontend-graph-canvas/high-level.md:113`: "Reduced zoom-dependent renderings were removed: they hid an…" and line 541 "because there are no longer two layouts to keep in sync". (Same R1 resolved-history violation class as low-level lines 414/445/792, but it occurs in the CONTEXT document owned by the other auditor; recorded here only for cross-document consistency.)
- frontend-graph-canvas `specs/frontend-graph-canvas/low-level.md:461`: The input-origin `::before` marker is described as "pale-accent" while the output marker's fill is described as 70% node accent / 30% `--text-primary`. (frontend/src/index.css:483-491 gives both directions the identical `color-mix(in srgb, currentColor 70%, var(--text-primary))` fill; whether "pale-accent" reflects a different `currentColor` set on the input handle in the component would need a per-call-site trace I did not run.)
- tracing `specs/tracing/low-level.md:558`: `ValueError` (missing/unselectable base frame) | `_build_parent_match_row` — the edge-join's base parent has no materialized output, is a multi-frame bundle with no wired/selectable frame handle (The raise sites are in `_edge_join_base_columns` (src/haute/_trace_correlation.py:1049-1056), which `_build_parent_match_row` calls; the attribution is one frame off but the propagation path and message content are as described. Not reported as a finding because the behaviour is accurate.)
- tracing `specs/tracing/high-level.md:299`: validated against `haute.schemas.TraceResultResponse` (The route validates the envelope `TraceResponse` (src/haute/routes/pipeline.py:978), whose `trace` field is typed `TraceResultResponse` (src/haute/schemas.py:908-910). Both symbols exist, so the claim is defensible; low-level.md:660 names `TraceResponse` for the golden test, which is what tests/test_trace_golden.py:8 imports.)
- sandbox-security `specs/sandbox-security/low-level.md:463`: "## Canonical sandbox payload ... Historical module aliases and shim globals are removed; allow-list and containment behavior for the current payload is unchanged." (Reads as resolved-history phrasing under R1 and adds a heading beyond the R4 set, but the same `## Canonical …` trailer appears in 8 low-level specs and is anchored to specs/README.md#canonical-only-format-policy (which resolves), so it looks like a sanctioned corpus-wide convention rather than a violation specific to this component.)
- sandbox-security `specs/sandbox-security/low-level.md:112`: "`executor._compile_preamble` calls `validate_user_code(..., allow_imports=True)` and then `exec()`s the preamble with `safe_globals(allow_imports=True)`" (Both calls exist but in neighbouring helpers, not in `_compile_preamble` itself: `validate_user_code(preamble, allow_imports=True)` is at src/haute/executor.py:478 (inside `_compile_preamble_into_cell`) and `safe_globals(pl=pl, allow_imports=True)` at src/haute/executor.py:369 (`_exec_preamble_namespace`). I did not trace the full cell/caching call chain to confirm `_compile_preamble` is the sole entry point, so this may be an accurate entry-point-level description.)
- sandbox-security `specs/sandbox-security/low-level.md:108`: "the execution engine's node-output check then raises `TypeError` if user code assigned `df` to a non-Polars value" (Not verified — I read `_user_exec.py` (which returns the value unchanged) but did not locate the execution-engine output type check that is claimed to raise `TypeError`.)
- server-api `specs/server-api/low-level.md:94`: `GraphEdge.sourceHandle`/`targetHandle` reject an empty string in a `field_validator` (The validator is real but is registered over four fields (`sourceHandle`, `targetHandle`, `sourcePort`, `targetPort`); `sourcePort`/`targetPort` are not described anywhere in this component's Key types section. I did not check whether the submodels spec documents those two fields, so this may be correct cross-component division rather than an omission.)
- server-api `specs/server-api/low-level.md:282`: Reject (close code 1008) if `websocket_rejection_reason` finds a problem with headers/query params (`websocket_rejection_reason` in src/haute/_local_security.py:325 inspects only headers; query parameters are explicitly not an auth transport (line 215 says so). The 'query params' clause looks vestigial, but I did not read the /ws/sync handler in full to rule out a separate query check.)
- databricks-io `specs/databricks-io/high-level.md:45`: "Resolved host and credential values never enter identity or metadata." (Verified for the identity descriptor (src/haute/_input_providers.py:98-110 stores only `host_ref`/`token_ref` symbols), but the descriptor records `token_ref: "DATABRICKS_TOKEN"` unconditionally even in service-principal mode; whether persisted source-cache metadata elsewhere echoes any resolved value was not traced through src/haute/_source_cache.py.)
- frontend-assistant-ui `specs/frontend-assistant-ui/high-level.md:115`: If a listed conversation disappears before it is opened (pruned or cleaned `.haute/`), the backend returns a fresh blank session; a 404 on *send* still renders the explicit session-expired notice. (The frontend half (404 on send → session-expired notice) is confirmed at frontend/src/stores/useAssistantStore.ts:229-235, but the backend's behaviour for a create with an unknown session id was not read in src/haute/assistant/_session.py.)
- frontend-assistant-ui `specs/frontend-assistant-ui/high-level.md:50`: Mutations-disabled (working branch not ready — no repository, unset, detached, divergent, or invalid) renders the same way, with the backend's per-state reason. (The frontend renders `mutations_reason` verbatim (useAssistantStore.ts:88); the exact five backend working-branch states were not enumerated from the backend source within budget.)
- frontend-assistant-ui `specs/frontend-assistant-ui/low-level.md:146`: Assistant text renders through a markdown renderer (GFM, raw HTML disabled) imported inside the lazy panel chunk so it never reaches the initial bundle. (react-markdown + remark-gfm imports confirmed in TranscriptEntryView.tsx:2-3 and lazy import confirmed at App.tsx:103, but the raw-HTML-disabled configuration and the bundle-boundary test assertion were not read.)
- build-and-distribution `specs/build-and-distribution/low-level.md:152`: `_run()` ... prints subprocess stdout/stderr, and raises `RuntimeError` on a non-zero return code or timeout; the missing-output sanity check does the same. (`_run()` prints stdout/stderr only on a non-zero return code (hatch_build.py:624-627), never on success or timeout, and I could not identify a distinct "missing-output sanity check" in hatch_build.py separate from `_require_static_readiness()`. The phrase may refer to readiness validation, so I could not confirm a contradiction rather than loose wording.)
- build-and-distribution `specs/build-and-distribution/low-level.md:74`: **MkDocs site inputs** are public Markdown, `docs/overrides/home.html`, `docs/stylesheets/extra.css`, and `mkdocs.yml` (The public Markdown guides under `docs/` (dozens of files named in mkdocs.yml's nav) are referred to as a group rather than enumerated or explicitly classified as corpus material per R5. Whether the coverage contract in specs/README.md exempts documentation content was not checked, so I did not raise it as a finding.)
- build-and-distribution `specs/build-and-distribution/low-level.md:46`: **Production-input manifest** is `src/haute/static/haute-build-inputs.json`. (That file is not present in the working tree's `src/haute/static/` (which holds assets/, favicon.svg, index.html, manifest.json, vite.svg), so the current checkout's static bundle would fail `_require_current_input_manifest`. This is build state rather than a spec error, but it means the packaged bundle on disk is not one the spec's validation would accept.)
- codegen `specs/codegen/low-level.md:133`: The `data_input` extraction matcher removes only its canonical imports, config-base setup, and load call on the reverse parse. (There is no `data_input` key in `BOILERPLATE_MATCHERS` (src/haute/_code_extraction.py:778-786 registers polars, explore, source, scenario_expander, model_score, rating_step, external); the data-input body is extracted under the `source` kind (src/haute/_code_extraction.py:1080). The sentence may be intended as "the matcher used for data_input nodes", so I did not treat the naming as a defect.)
- codegen `specs/codegen/low-level.md:36`: `validate_pipeline_graph_shape_contracts` — structural preflight (owned by `haute._graph_shape`), run before any source is generated. (True for the flat path (src/haute/codegen.py:1260), but the submodel path calls a differently named `validate_graph_shape_contracts` (src/haute/codegen.py:1028) after the unreferenced-definition and shared-file checks. I did not confirm whether the two names are aliases in `_graph_shape.py`, so I did not raise it as a stale-symbol finding.)
- codegen `specs/codegen/low-level.md:369`: `_save_pipeline._validate_transforms_are_runnable` (The symbol is a method on a class rather than a module-level function (src/haute/routes/_save_pipeline.py:1377 `def _validate_transforms_are_runnable(self, graph, warnings)`), so the `module.symbol` spelling is imprecise; I judged this below the reporting bar rather than a resolution failure.)
- reference-pipeline `specs/reference-pipeline/low-level.md:9`: `rating/main.py` is named in prose by specs/cli/low-level.md:84 and specs/pipeline-config/low-level.md:191 while specs/ownership.toml records no rating/ entries (R8 prose-ownership discipline). (Those mentions read as scaffold/interaction description rather than ownership claims, and tests/docs_accuracy_baseline.txt carries no reference-pipeline debt line, suggesting the accuracy ratchet already accepts them; I did not read the ratchet's ownership rule in full.)
- reference-pipeline `specs/reference-pipeline/low-level.md:59`: "Parsing `rating/main.py` loads and validates both decorators' referenced sidecars." (The parser does load configs and marks `_load_error` on failure, but I did not trace the node-builder path that performs the load to confirm that both the api_input and output sidecars are read and schema-validated (as opposed to merely read as JSON) at parse time.)
